# P4 — Engine & Bundling Framework

> **The reusable harness every format engine plugs into.** P4 builds the generic
> engine-invocation layer, per-OS sidecar packaging/bundling, the §2.12 decoder-
> isolation boundary, the §0.9 subprocess pool, the cross-cutting reliability test
> machinery (per-pair runner + pair-status ledger + corpus↔pair bijection guard), the
> SBOM/NOTICE scaffold, the §7.2.3 startup engine-presence/integrity verifier, the §3.4
> patent-disposition matrix + availability wiring, the §3.9 size-budget levers, and the
> **generic UX-correctness primitives** (options-panel shell, lossy notes, progress/
> cancel, result-actions, error copy, structural a11y) — so each later engine phase
> (P5–P7) registers per-format declarations against an already-built UI and reaches its
> §6.5 `reliable` gate without waiting on P8.
>
> Spec home: 01-conversion-pipeline (§1.7 the generic engine-invocation lifecycle —
> P4.6–P4.12; §1.10 the resource pre-flight & budgets engine — P4.72/P4.73),
> 03-engines-and-bundling (engine-invocation layer, per-OS bundling, image-
> worker `convertia-imgworker`, §3.4 patent matrix + §3.4.4a availability wiring, §3.5.0
> macOS TCC staging, §3.9 size levers), 02-guarantees (§2.12 isolation, §0.9 pool,
> §2.12.3 privilege-drop, §0.11 threat-map, §2.13 app-fault, §2.8/§2.9 UX primitives),
> 06-build-test-release (§6.4.3/§6.4.3a/§6.5 reliability machinery, SBOM scaffold, §6.1.3
> build assertions), 07-app-shell (§7.2.3 integrity manifest + startup verifier, §7.2.6
> macOS TCC), 05-ui-ux (generic UX primitives). Index: [README.md](README.md). Box
> format: [`_format.md`](_format.md).
>
> **Exit criterion (proof-of-life):** `convertia-imgworker` boots, a round-trip
> invocation succeeds through the §2.12 isolation boundary, the startup verifier reports
> a populated `EngineHealth`, the §6.4.3 runner + pair-status ledger + §6.4.3a bijection
> guard produce their first report, **and** a representative **in-P4 image round-trip**
> (a P4-staged minimal fixture + throwaway pair, NOT a P5 corpus pair — so P4 is
> self-contained, no P4→P5 inversion) is driven end-to-end through the P4-built
> options-panel shell + progress/cancel + result-actions UI to a first ledger cell over
> the P4 fixture (the UX-harness leg — P4 is not "done" on the engine side alone).
>
> **This is the v0 base** — atomic `[ ]` boxes below; a later adversarial review will
> deepen, split and complete it. P4 does **not** re-implement `crate::fs_guard` (built in
> P3); it fills the `crate::isolation` + pool shells P3 established. Per-engine SSRF/LFR
> hardening (FFmpeg/pandoc/LibreOffice/librsvg) lives in P5/P6/P7, not here; per-engine
> SBOM rows / §6.1.3 assertion lists / §7.2.3 availability rows are populated by P5–P7
> against the generic frameworks built here.

---

- [x] **P4.11** [RUST] Build the kill↔cleanup↔no-partial ordering + the bounded confirm-wait + deferred-reclaim residue path · §1.7 §2.6 · G29 G31
  needs: P4.10
  > the §1.7 ordering: signal cancel → group-kill + **timeout-bounded** confirm-wait (so a wedged descendant cannot hang the UI/quit) → on-timeout defer temp reclaim to the §2.6 sweep AND carry `CleanupResidue` (the §2.8.2 "With residue" tail, never a silent leftover) → §2.6 cleanup of the per-job temp → mark Cancelled/Failed and continue the queue (§1.9). Already-`Succeeded` items untouched.
  > **[Forward note from P4.10, 2026-07-23]** the confirm-wait this box BOUNDS is not a group-drain on Windows: `process-wrap` 9.1.0's `JobObjectChild::wait` returns on the FIRST completion-port message rather than on `JOB_OBJECT_MSG_ACTIVE_PROCESS_ZERO`, so an `Ok(status)` does NOT prove the Job Object is empty; POSIX's `ProcessGroupChild::wait` is no better: `waitpid(-pgid)` → `ECHILD` only means WE have no children left in that group, and a grandchild was never our child, so it proves nothing about live descendants either. The bound must therefore be built as "wait up to T for the group to settle, then defer", never as "the crate's wait returned, so the group is gone". P4.10 already issues the whole-group kill on every invocation path that ends WITHOUT a completed engine wait — cancel, failed reap, early return, and the future being dropped (`GroupKillGuard`); after a COMPLETED wait the guard stands down by decision (see its `Drop`). So this box adds the BOUND + the `CleanupResidue` tail, not the kill. **Coverage note:** the guard's DECISION is pinned in both directions by the `crate::isolation::confined_spawn_tests` guard test, and its UNSETTLED arms end-to-end by the two teardown tests there; the single `group_settled = true` assignment in the completed-wait arm is covered by INSPECTION only (an end-to-end stand-down assertion is structurally vacuous on Windows — a `start /b` grandchild inherits the invocation's stdout/stderr PIPE handles, so the drain cannot reach EOF while the descendant runs, measured across five cmd redirection variants). This box adds the bounded confirm-wait (see the RECONCILE below for how its coverage actually lands).
  > **[RECONCILE — delivered 2026-07-24, P4.11]** the "REWRITES *that arm*" anticipation above (the P4.10-authored forecast) was IMPRECISE: the confirm-wait belongs on the **cancel arm**, NOT the completed-wait (normal-exit) arm. Per spec §1.7 the confirm-wait is a *cancel*-path step (kill → wait), and bolting one onto the successful-completion arm would truncate a launcher-exits-early worker — the exact P4.10 stand-down regression `GroupKillGuard`'s Drop exists to prevent. So the completed-wait arm's `group_settled = true` **stays INSPECTION-covered** (as P4.10 established), and P4.11 instead adds an executable test of the settle logic via the new isolation confirm-wait test + the cancel-path tests. Delivered accordingly; the forecast's "close the coverage of *that arm*" does not apply — the completed-wait arm is deliberately untouched.

The pin stays 9.1.0 (10.0.0 is a breaking major; the bump = a deliberate dep-major act of its own with fresh review + the §0.8 floor move — spec §1.7 carries the `[UPSTREAM]` notes); this box's own Job Object stays the in-product close either way.
  > **[Forward note from P4.16, 2026-07-25 — the fork-child-hang defect class (prevention sweep of the P4.16 Co-Pilot ruling)]** the P4.16 ruling forbade the macOS Seatbelt apply leg because its only apply path runs in the **post-fork/pre-exec child** (a non-silent-skippable HANG risk — not auditable fork-safe). The Windows tier is a **CHECKED NON-MEMBER** of that class: a restricted-token / AppContainer / Job-Object is applied **PARENT-SIDE at CreateProcess time** (process-creation attributes / `process-wrap`), NOT in a post-fork child, and a setup failure is an errno-like error the wrapper silently skips to the cheap tier — never a fork-child hang. So P4.17 keeps its best-effort tier; the constraint it inherits is only that it must apply at **spawn/creation time (parent-side)**, never via a post-spawn/hang-risky path.
  > **[Decision: P4.17 — Co-Pilot ruling 2026-08-25 (rev. after the G1 round-1 review), anchor cleanest-most-complete; resolves the Loop's four-mechanism realizability escalation]** RE-CUTS the deliverable above to the two mechanisms that ARE realizable in v1-portable, REVERSES the escalation's low-integrity verdict on first-hand evidence, and — closing the round-1 Opus P0 — realizes the write-confinement leg at a **ConvertIA-private intermediate integrity level, NOT the well-known Low**. Verdicts: (1) **restricted token / AppContainer — UNREALIZABLE (confirmed, on stronger ground):** stable `std::os::windows::process::CommandExt` has no `raw_attribute` / token parameter (Co-Pilot compile-probe on rustc 1.96.0: E0599) and `tokio::process::Child` cannot be built from a raw handle; AND independently of any spawn-token API, an AppContainer needs `ALL APPLICATION PACKAGES` DACL grants on the engine bundle dir (a runtime ACL mutation of the portable install dir — impossible on a FAT/exFAT stick) and on every input (source-metadata mutation = §2.0 harm, or a Windows source-staging copy) — so the revisit anchor is an **installer-build epoch plus a brokered/staged input model**, NOT merely a stabilized `raw_attribute`. (2) **reduced-integrity token — REALIZABLE, BUILD IT (reversal):** the escalation was right that a lowered child cannot write a Medium-labelled sink and wrong that the sinks are fixed — every engine write sink is OURS (the parent `create_new`s the §2.14.1 `.part`; the §2.14.2 kind-2 scratch is app-owned), so the parent labels them BEFORE lowering the token: the Windows analogue of the Landlock `{scratch rw}` grant (P4.15.1). Co-Pilot probe (this machine, NTFS): `CreateProcess(CREATE_SUSPENDED)` → `OpenProcessToken(TOKEN_ADJUST_DEFAULT|TOKEN_QUERY)` → `SetTokenInformation(TokenIntegrityLevel)` = TRUE and the child ran; the lowered child could write a same-labelled sink, was DENIED on a Medium sink, and still READ a Medium source; the explicit label TRAVELS across a same-volume `MoveFileEx` and the Medium parent can strip it afterwards. **The round-1 Opus P0 (a real find, fixed here, not recorded-around):** labelling the `.part` at the well-known **Low** IL would make it writable by EVERY same-user process running at Low — Acrobat's renderer, Office Protected View, browser content processes, i.e. the exact sandboxes a hostile document compromises — which, mid-conversion, could overwrite the `.part`; we then strip and publish the attacker's bytes under the expected name. FIX (class-level, A2): label the sink set at a **ConvertIA-private mandatory IL strictly between Low (0x1000) and Medium (0x2000) — the canonical `0x1800` (6144)** — and lower the engine token to it. Then a **Low (4096) co-tenant is denied write-UP to a 6144 object** (`NO_WRITE_UP`, the MIC total order), so the standard Low sandboxes are excluded by construction; the engine (6144) still **cannot write Medium (8192) user files** (the tier's actual goal is preserved); the Medium parent can still write + strip. Co-Pilot probe (no-child API): `S-1-16-6144` is settable as a mandatory label and reads back **verbatim** `S:(ML;;NW;;;S-1-16-6144)` (Windows keeps the exact RID, does not snap it to a well-known level), the strip (empty SACL) reads back `NO_ACCESS_CONTROL`; the MIC total-order ordering was measured first-hand at the Low/Medium endpoints (Low→Low write OK, Low→Medium write DENIED, both reviewers independently reproduced), and Windows' own **Medium Plus (0x2100)** proves intermediate RIDs are kernel-enforced numerically, not table-limited. §2.14.1's mode-bits clause ("default per-user ACL … no explicit broadening") is DACL-/confidentiality-scoped (the `0o600`/`0o700` mirror): a mandatory LABEL leaves the DACL untouched and is the orthogonal integrity dimension, so this is a CLARIFICATION not a collision; and §2.12.3 mandates the tier "where it works WITHOUT install-time elevation and without breaking the portable build", which the intermediate-IL leg does. (3) **Job-Object caps — BUILD (confirmed).** (4) **AppContainer/WFP net-deny — UNREALIZABLE (confirmed):** WFP/firewall needs elevation and leaves a persistent machine-global mutation; the AppContainer half falls with (1); revisit anchor = an installer-build epoch. **DELIVERABLE — two legs, both PARENT-SIDE and PRE-RESUME (the P4.16 forward note's constraint), raw Win32 in `crate::platform` behind a safe shim (`windows-sys` feature-adds `Win32_Security` / `Win32_Security_Authorization` / `Win32_System_JobObjects` / `Win32_System_Threading` are Loop-doable, no new dep):** **Leg A — intermediate-IL write confinement.** (i) LABEL-THEN-LOWER, never the reverse: before the spawn the parent sets an explicit `0x1800` mandatory label on the child's write set — which is the **full §2.14.1/§2.14.3 placement sink set the OutputPlan actually chose**, NOT a fixed pair: the per-run scratch cwd (`(OI)(CI)`, so the LibreOffice `--outdir`/profile/TMP subtree inherits it), the `.part` sibling in the destination dir, and — on the §2.14.3 fallback — the destination-dir parent and/or the other-volume central-scratch intermediate; `GetVolumeInformationW` is evaluated **per sink** (the other-volume intermediate sits on a DIFFERENT volume), and the token is lowered ONLY if every sink lies on a `FILE_PERSISTENT_ACLS` volume AND every label call succeeded, else that spawn runs the cheap tier (a FAT/exFAT-stick destination, a Modify-only folder without `WRITE_OWNER`, an SMB share) — so no FAT/SMB integrity-enforcement assumption is load-bearing; the lowering is also skipped if the source or the engine binary carries an explicit label at or above the chosen IL with `NO_READ_UP`/`NO_EXECUTE_UP`. The label is set **non-recursively (no `/T`-equivalent)** so the pre-existing `run-<RunId>/.lock` (created lock-before-part, §2.6.3) keeps its implicit Medium — and even if it were labelled, a Medium parent still accesses a lower-IL object, so nothing breaks. (ii) APPLY in an own `process_wrap::CommandWrapper` `post_spawn` hook (verified against process-wrap 9.1.0 `generic_wrap.rs`: every `post_spawn` runs before any `wrap_child`, and `JobObject::wrap_child` is what resumes the `CREATE_SUSPENDED` threads — the child is still suspended there): `OpenProcessToken` → `SetTokenInformation(TokenIntegrityLevel)`; **any failure MUST silent-skip, NEVER return `Err`** — a `post_spawn` `Err` propagates with `?` BEFORE any `wrap_child`, so the `JobObject` wrapper never resumes the threads and the `CREATE_SUSPENDED` child is dropped un-resumed + un-reaped (a stranded orphan + a broken conversion); pinned by a red-green test ("a forced `SetTokenInformation` failure still yields a resumed, completing child"). The Applied signal is a parent-side `GetTokenInformation` read-back of the child token's level (the grant-is-enforcement model) feeding the P4.18 record. (iii) LABEL STRIP BEFORE PUBLISH: after the engine exit + the §1.7 non-empty verification and BEFORE the §2.1.2 move, the parent REMOVES the explicit label from the `.part` (`SetNamedSecurityInfoW` with `LABEL_SECURITY_INFORMATION` and a SACL without a label ACE — not "set Medium"; probe: reads back `NO_ACCESS_CONTROL`), so `final` carries the destination's implicit level; test = a SACL read-back of `final` shows no label ACE. A strip failure copies the bytes into a fresh `create_new` sibling and publishes THAT — never the source, never an existing `final`. ONE strip site covers every engine (the LibreOffice outdir→`.part` hand-over and the §2.14.3 cross-volume copy both end in the `.part`). (iv) The delete-then-recreate engine pattern (FFmpeg truncate-vs-recreate) is NOT a Landlock-style tuning residual: on Windows a lowered child that DELETEs then re-creates the `.part` succeeds the unlink but is DENIED the re-create in the Medium destination dir, and the unlink RELEASES the parent's §2.1.2 `create_new`'d reserved name mid-run — closing this would require labelling the user's whole destination DIRECTORY (unacceptable), so the G31/G32 corpus outcome for such an engine routes to **"skip the intermediate-IL leg for this engine" (a per-engine tier decision)**, never "tune the grant". The enforcement claim (a Low co-tenant is denied write-UP to the 6144 `.part`) is armed by the Loop's build-time red-green test on a **real Windows CI runner** (the grant-is-enforcement model, child-observed, exactly as the Landlock/seccomp/net-ns legs are proven) — the Co-Pilot verified the label settability + the MIC ordering endpoints first-hand; the runner test arms the full claim. **Leg B — the OWN Job Object** (as escalated): `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` (closes the P4.10 crash-time-reap residual) + `JOB_MEMORY` (a runaway guard, generous — e.g. a fraction of physical RAM with a floor; the value is `[DEFER: tuning]`) + `ACTIVE_PROCESS` (a fork-bomb guard, generous) + `DIE_ON_UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION` (no WER dialog can hold a crashed engine until the watchdog); assigned in the same `post_spawn` before the resume; CLEAR `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` **only on the CLEAN-exit arm** (the launcher-outlives-worker case) before the handle closes — genuinely matching the delivered `crate::isolation::GroupKillGuard` stand-down: the shipped P4.12 code (`isolation/mod.rs`) sets `group_settled = true` and stands the guard DOWN **only** on a clean completed wait, while a **CRASH completed wait leaves `group_settled` false so the guard FIRES the explicit group-kill** (the tree is doomed). So Leg B mirrors that — on the crash arm **LEAVE `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` SET** as a redundant OS-level host-crash backstop behind that explicit kill, closing the P4.10 crash-time-reap residual on exactly the arm where the tree should die (clearing it there would re-open a window where a ConvertIA-host crash leaves the engine tree unreaped). This is CONSISTENT with the existing crash-kills-the-tree semantics, so it does NOT touch the P4.12-parked group-kill-POLICY question. Exactly ONE kill-on-close / cap authority: either NESTED (ours in `post_spawn`; the crate's `wrap_child` job becomes its child job — Windows 8+ nested jobs) or REPLACE the crate's `JobObject` wrapper — the Loop's build choice, under the constraint that the P4.10/P4.11 group-kill + wait contract survives 1:1 and a test pins nested-vs-single (`TerminateJobObject` on ours reaps the tree; a cap breach → item `Failed`, batch continues — P4.18.2). **Alternative weighed + rejected (round-1 Sonnet):** keeping `out_tmp` in a lowered per-run SCRATCH only + a parent scratch→`.part` copy is DOMINATED by the intermediate-IL leg — it costs the §2.14.1 same-volume no-copy optimization AND only RELOCATES the lowered object into `%LOCALAPPDATA%` scratch (still co-tenant-writable there), whereas the intermediate IL keeps the `.part` beside `final` per §2.14.1 AND closes the co-tenant hole outright. **Pins:** a cross-platform source-scan (the `no_seatbelt_apply_callsite_in_the_core` sibling) asserting no AppContainer / `CreateProcessAsUserW` / WFP FFI enters the core (no dead code for a decided-not-applied mechanism). **Sync split — DECISION-sync LANDS IN THE RULING COMMIT (this commit, the P4.16 precedent), IMPLEMENTATION-sync is the Loop's P4.17 build commit:** landing NOW = spec §2.12.3 Windows bullet `[DECIDED — P4.17]` (realized = intermediate-IL + Job Object; unrealizable = restricted token/AppContainer + net-deny, with the grounds + the two revisit anchors) + the §2.14.1 label clarification (DACL untouched; the mandatory label is the orthogonal integrity dimension = the Windows analogue of the Landlock rw grant; the intermediate IL keeps Low co-tenants out) + the security-concept §5 residual note (below) + the frozen P4.16-box reconcile pointer + the two L(-1) G42b/T9b rows; the Loop's build commit adds the §2.1.1/§2.1.2 Windows strip step spec text + the code + the executable runner test + the AppContainer-naming SWEEP of the spec README §-summaries and the `crate::isolation` module docs (the mechanism-recut class). **NON-MEMBERS of that sweep (recorded, A2):** P9.27.5 + §6.7.3 CI net-deny gates use an ELEVATED runner firewall (a CI fact) and STAND unchanged; and the network-confinement AppContainer mentions are a SEPARATE axis whose load-bearing gate is the §2.11.4 packet-monitor regardless of tier — the ruling narrows the privilege-drop AppContainer + the runtime net-deny, not the CI gate. **Threat residual (round-1 Opus, DoD-8 spirit):** the deliberate intermediate-IL label on the `.part`/scratch is recorded as an honest residual of THIS §2.12.3 control in the spec §2.12.3 note (its authoritative home — the §5 T9b cell is READ-scoped and records the read-half (b)-residual only), NOT a new §0.11 T-class — with the 6144 choice the residual shrinks to "a same-user process running at an unusual IL in (Low, Medium)", a same-user CROSS-INTEGRITY co-tenancy residual (distinct from the CROSS-user T13 — not that class, so it does not borrow its precedent) OUT of the single-user offline model, whose compensating controls are: standard Low sandboxes are excluded by the intermediate IL, the DACL is not the defence, the label is stripped before publish, and the tier is best-effort non-load-bearing (even total absence breaks no guarantee — the T9b/offline guarantees rest on the §3.5/§6.1.3 argv/build controls + the §2.11.4 packet gate). A dedicated §0.11 T-class instead of this control-residual note is a clean **owner follow-up** if preferred (recording the judgment, not silently choosing the smaller option). And the P4.16 forward note directly above is CORRECTED by this ruling: its premise that the Windows tier applies at CreateProcess time via process-creation attributes / process-wrap does not hold on this stack — the realizable Windows legs apply POST-creation PRE-resume (still parent-side, still never a fork child), which is what the constraint actually requires. **G42b (L(-1), delivered by the Co-Pilot in this commit):** the intermediate-IL leg confines WRITES only (files carry no `NO_READ_UP`) and the Job Object confines nothing on the read side, so the read-half option (a) is unreachable on Windows exactly as on macOS → the G42b row's Windows menu is narrowed to **(b) by decision** and its "P4.17 decides" clause resolved; the §5 T9b cell mirrors it. - [x] **P4.20** [RUST] Expand the P3 pool shell into the bounded engine-subprocess pool + global concurrency degree · §0.9 · G29

P4 fills the shell P3 established (does not build from scratch). **[Reconcile: P4.20 — REUSE P3.3's `crate::pool::clamp_global_degree`, do NOT re-inline the formula]** the `clamp(physical_cores−1, 1, 4)` literal is P3.3's owned formula, resolved via `std::thread::available_parallelism()` (§0.9 `[DECIDED: P3.3]` — std exposes no physical-core API); P4.20 calls `clamp_global_degree` verbatim and layers the `effective = min(global_degree, per_engine_cap, memory_based_cap)` §1.10 factor on top, never re-authoring the degree formula. (IF this leg promotes a new direct dep, its §0.8 floor row lands via P4.84 (landed 2026-09-15) — the dep↔floor split; the already-floored `rustix`/`windows-sys` need no new row.)
  > **Delivered (P4.20) [Build-Session-Entscheidung: P4.20]:** the shelled SUBPROCESS half is filled as `Pool::run_subprocess` — take a slot (`slot_weight` permits: ONE at full effective degree, more under §1.10 memory pressure), await the caller's engine future, release on EVERY exit path. The §1.10 watermark pause is deliberately NOT in either lane (see below), so a P4.32 caller must not re-add it there. It takes a caller-supplied FUTURE (not the in-core lane's `'static` closure) because `crate::isolation::run_confined` and the §1.7 `bounded_confined_run` BORROW their arguments and carry no `Send`/`'static` bound; staying generic in the caller's `R` is also what keeps this §0.7 tier-3 leaf from naming a single tier-2 type. `clamp_global_degree` is REUSED verbatim per the Reconcile note (never re-inlined). `Pool::effective_degree(per_engine_cap)` is the §0.9/§1.10 three-term `min(global_degree, per_engine_cap, memory_based_cap)` — the frame P4.21 passes its caps into; the memory term is `available / MEMORY_PER_SLOT_BYTES` floored at 1, so too little memory for one slot is SERIAL exactly as §1.10 requires. The effective degree is ENFORCED, not merely computed: `slot_weight` turns it into the number of global permits one slot takes (`ceil(degree / effective)`), so under memory pressure the same fixed semaphore admits proportionally fewer lanes — down to exactly one, §1.10's "down to serial" (a live semaphore cannot be resized without racing in-flight holders; where `degree` is not a multiple of `effective` the integer weight under-admits by one, the safe direction). The high-memory watermark holds a NEW item at the §1.7 DISPATCH ENTRY (`crate::engines::dispatch`), CANCEL-AWARE (a `biased` select on the job token, so a C7 cancel — and via §7.3.3 a quit — is honoured at once instead of after the ceiling, while the bias keeps an unpaused dispatch on its pre-existing §1.9 path) and deliberately OUTSIDE both lanes: §1.7 wraps each lane in a wall-clock timeout, so a pause spent inside one would come out of the ENGINE's budget and could turn a slow-but-progressing conversion into `Failed(EngineHang)` — a defence-in-depth throttle becoming the reason a conversion fails, which §2.12.3's never-break floor forbids. That placement also makes "in-flight items finish" hold by construction, and matches §1.10's own wording (it pauses DISPATCH, it does not shorten a running engine). The pause re-reads every `MEMORY_WATERMARK_POLL` and is CEILED at `MEMORY_PAUSE_MAX` for the same never-break reason; on expiry the §1.10 per-item ceiling takes over. The gate has a LIVE production consumer today (dispatch's in-core CSV/TSV lane), not merely a P4.32 one. **The memory reading is a new `crate::platform` OS shim** `available_memory_bytes` (the free-DISK `available_bytes` sibling, built at its first need so the read has ONE home): Linux `/proc/meminfo` `MemAvailable` (pure std, no FFI), Windows `GlobalMemoryStatusEx` (a windows-sys FEATURE add — no package, no lock change), macOS Mach `host_statistics64(HOST_VM_INFO64)` + `sysconf(_SC_PAGESIZE)` (a `libc` macOS target-table row — libc is already a direct dep, so the lock is byte-identical). Its signature is `Option<u64>`, the OPPOSITE failure bias to `available_bytes`'s `io::Result`: an unreadable free-disk figure must fail toward "does not fit", while an unreadable MEMORY figure must impose no cap at all. macOS publishes no `MemAvailable` equivalent, so the page-count combination is a decision — `free + inactive`, the two counts that are unambiguously disjoint and reclaimable without swapping or compressing; `speculative` is excluded (already inside `free_count`), `compressor`/`external` are excluded (reclaiming those IS the pressure the gate avoids), and `purgeable` is excluded because XNU also counts purgeable pages on the active/inactive queues, so including it would double-count an unknown overlap and could make the figure OVER-report. The result under-reports by construction, the safe direction; P6.56.1's memory-constrained-host run validates it against a real Mac rather than by argument. Any single consistent order is deadlock-free — there is exactly one lane-acquire site and one `dispatch` site, so no inversion exists — but only this one cannot wedge throughput: global-first would let N queued office jobs each hold a global permit while waiting on the single office permit, so an office-heavy batch could fill the §0.9 degree with waiters and starve every other engine. The tier-1 conductor holds the engine guard across `dispatch` (which takes the global permit inside its lane) and NOT across the §2.1 publish, so both are released the instant the engine run ends — success, failure, kill, cancel or a dropped future. A bonus the placement earns: the office wait sits OUTSIDE the §1.7 wall-clock window, so P4.86's "the timeout clock starts at permit GRANT" obligation already holds on this axis. An `Err` (unreachable — the lanes expose no close) fails THAT ONE item closed rather than running an office job whose serialisation could not be established. Retuning a P4.12 test's timing model from inside a P4.23 verify box was declined as out of scope and as the "no green-by-rewrite" anti-pattern; BOTH reviewers agreed. But test-strategy §7 is stricter than a shrug ("unit / property / integration / fuzz levels NEVER auto-retry; a red there is a real finding" — "engineer determinism instead of retrying … freeze any clock the assertion reads"), so a real-clock, real-subprocess test with a 10s trigger is a determinism debt §7 says to engineer out. Nothing about copying a file into a scratch dir is macOS-specific; only the decision to do it is. That split is what makes the box verifiable at all — the mechanism compiles and is regression-tested on all three CI legs AND on the Build-Loop's own host, leaving ~1 line unverifiable off-mac, where a module-level `#![cfg(target_os = "macos")]` would have hidden the entire slice from two of three gate-tooling legs and from the author. **PROBED, not assumed** (the `os-mechanism-realizability-needs-empirical-probe` rule): `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` was tried and FAILS on this Windows host — a `-sys` dependency needs an Apple `cc` — so there is no local macOS validation path at all, which is precisely why the untestable surface had to be minimised rather than accepted. **Homing:** the module is declared UNCONDITIONALLY in `isolation/mod.rs`, because a `#[cfg(target_os = "macos")] mod macos;` there would ITSELF be a mac-cfg outside `macos.rs` and would fail check-sast's own `misplaced_macos_cfg` leg (the P4.85 homing contract); per-item `#[cfg]` inside the file is the `crate::platform` pattern.   > **THE §6.4.2 KILL FENCE, delivered rather than argued (sonnet r1 P2, opus r1 P2).** §2.14.2/§6.4.2 enumerate a specific case — *kill the app between the staged source copy and the engine spawn; on next launch assert the staged copy and its `run-<RunId>/` dir are reclaimed by the startup sweep* — and both reviewers found it homed in NO plan box. The containment argument above is sound and opus verified both halves independently, but §6.4.2 asks for the test, so the box now carries it: `a_staged_source_left_by_a_crash_is_reclaimed_by_the_next_launch_sweep`, homed in `crate::run`'s `sweep_tests` — beside the free-lock sweep siblings whose grace bypass it reuses — because `sweep_stale_within` is private there. The crash is modelled as §2.6.3 defines death — by DROPPING the `RunScratch` (no `Drop` impl, so nothing is cleaned on the way out), leaving the dir exactly as a killed process would. The case is now OWNED here, not forward-declared.
  >
  > **§2.12.4 pre-open regular-file check (opus r1 P1 — a real in-core hang).** `fs::copy` OPENS the untrusted source, and std's own guard runs AFTER that open, so a FIFO/pipe/device would park the copy forever IN-CORE — before any spawn, where §1.7's subprocess timeouts cannot see it, over exactly the Desktop/Documents/Downloads paths §7.2.6 is about. Staging now runs `orchestrator::detect_candidate`'s P3.49 pre-check (the same owner-accepted µs stat→open residual) and returns an honest `ErrorKind::InvalidInput`. The repo had ruled on this class twice before and staging matched neither: `detect_candidate` PERFORMS the check, `csv_tsv_transform` DOCUMENTS the precondition; staging did neither while having by far the widest window (freeze → queue → staging).
  >
  > **THE macOS DEAD-CODE TRAP (opus r1 P0 — it would have reddened the macos leg).** `stage_for_tcc` first carried `#[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]`, which expands to NOTHING in a test build — and `cargo clippy --all-targets` on the macos-14 leg compiles the lib test target, where `pub(crate)` is no dead_code root and no test called the fn. The allowance is now UNCONDITIONAL (an `allow` can never go unfulfilled), and the delegation test gives it a caller besides. The sibling sweep was run and confirms this was the UNIQUE instance: the five `cfg(target_os = "linux")` items in `crate::platform` carrying the same `not(test)` form all have a caller on their own target — `landlock_probe`/`seccomp_probe` from cfg-gated tests, `netns_verdict`/`spawn_leg_verdicts` from PRODUCTION (`isolation/mod.rs`), `ATTACHED_LEGS` from both — so the `not(test)` form is correct there and only a target-gated item with ZERO callers on its target needs the unconditional form. The `platform/mod.rs:948` precedent this box cited states the same rule in its other shape (`any(not(test), target_os = …)`): allow unconditionally on the target where the item is dead under BOTH cfgs. Catcher: a Loop-memory entry.
  >
  > **The mechanism is MODULE-PRIVATE (opus r1 P2).** §3.5.0 scopes staging to macOS and §2.14.4 puts the kind-2 staged-source term at 0 on Windows/Linux, so a crate-visible `stage_source_into_scratch` would let a later box add an unmodelled kind-2 footprint off-macOS that the §1.10 preflight does not account for — and G29 rule (d) would not catch it. Module privacy closes that structurally; the kill-fence test reaches the mechanism through a `#[cfg(test)]`-only door that no production build contains.
  >
  > **`· G31` is NOT delivered by this box** (opus r1 P3, stated so a later audit does not read it as satisfied): G31's macOS T11 sub-test — the Rust core PID, not the engine PID, is the first accessor, and the engine receives a kind-2 scratch path — needs a real spawn, which is P4.25/P4.26. The nine tests here are §6.4.1 G15 units plus the one §6.4.2 G31 property (the kill fence).
  >
  > **ROUND 2 — the two findings worth keeping (opus; sonnet GO'd the same tree).** (1) **A GUARD TEST THAT PROVED NOTHING WHERE IT MATTERED.** The §2.12.4 pre-check's test asserted only `ErrorKind::InvalidInput` — but std's `fs::copy` opens FIRST and checks `is_file()` after, returning the *identical* `InvalidInput` for a directory. So deleting the pre-check would have left the test green on Linux and macOS, i.e. on exactly the two platforms where the in-core FIFO hang is real; only Windows (`CopyFileExW` → `PermissionDenied`) would have caught the regression. The test now also asserts the message carries our own `§2.12.4` tag, which is what proves the PRE-open guard fired. The general lesson: when a guard returns the same error the unguarded path would, the ErrorKind is not evidence — the test must be able to tell OUR refusal from the library's. (2) **A CORRECTION THAT WAS ONLY HALF SWEPT.** The pandoc fix landed in the fn doc and in this note but MISSED the same claim 165 lines below in a test comment — leaving the file self-contradicting, with the surviving half being the one already ruled false, under a `[Corrected by …]` tag whose own wording then read untrue. That is the `doc-staleness-sweep-discipline` rule (sweep a class to TRUE zero in ONE pass, grep every phrasing) failing on a two-site class. Both sites are now correct. Also from round 2: the kill fence had landed in `parallel_scratch_binding_tests` while this note claimed it sat beside its sweep siblings — moved to `sweep_tests`, where the claim is true and the sibling citation is a local module anchor rather than a cross-module bare `#[test] fn` name (the `rs-comment-test-fn-ref-module-anchor` rule, which `G73` cannot enforce here since `check-rs-test-refs` scans only `ipc/**`).
  > **ROUND 3 — what this box RAISED, and the note-vs-mechanism lesson.** Verifying the kill fence turned up a SECOND unowned §2.14.2 obligation: staged copies must be reclaimed **per item** as each engine finishes (`[DECIDED]`, and the sole justification for §1.10's peak-concurrent bound over the whole-batch Σ), and nothing in production does it — neither existing reclaimer is per-item (the §2.6.2 run-scope cleanup fires at run end, the §2.6.3 sweep at the NEXT launch). It is not this box's: `stage_source_into_scratch` has ZERO production callers, so there is no runtime point here at which "the engine finished" exists. **It is now P4.88, `needs: P4.25`, blocking P4.72 through that box's own `needs:`.** My first attempt recorded it as prose in two `>`-notes, and BOTH reviewers rejected that: one of the notes offered "model Σ" as a fallback, which §2.14.2 `[DECIDED]` forbids and P4.72's own scope line already forbade three rows below it — a plan-over-spec inversion authored in the very commit that was fixing one — under a guard ("until P4.25's reclaim is `[x]`") that no `[x]` could ever satisfy. The lesson, now a memory entry: **a normative obligation you have just discovered is unimplemented needs a BOX and a `needs:` edge, not a note.** plan-lint's dependency graph enforces; prose does not.

The enforcement plane stays L(-1)/gate-side regardless of the script's author: the pins live in the caged `engines.lock`, the G37 restore-re-verify step (which the G56 workflow-lint sub-rule requires between every cache restore and `stage-engines`) re-verifies every byte the script produced, and the SHA-pinned `actions/cache` wiring is the `.github/**` half — so a weakened fetch script cannot ship an unpinned byte. The EGRESS axis is bound the same way: the script's fetch targets are READ from the caged `engines.lock` rows — whose pin- and corroboration-URLs the P4.56.3 allow-list gate polices — so WHERE it may connect is L(-1) data, not script code, and the `g24-fetch-engine-assets.py` canary plants an off-`engines.lock`-URL positive. The script is deliberately NOT cage-homed at authoring; its cage membership is ruled by the OWNER at this box's own owner-acked workflow-tail act (recorded there — the natural checkpoint, with the script in hand; the P4.41/P4.51 rulings are scoped verbatim to `scripts/stage-engines` and do not cover this one). `stage-engines` stays zero-network; `fetch-engine-assets` becomes the ONLY sanctioned network actor at package time, and only on a cache miss. **Sequencing (the `_format.md` §6 machinery order — DECISION C recurses into an un-built `needs:` target and completes it, owner-acked tail and check-off included, before returning):** P4.56.1 in FULL first (its Loop half, then its owner-acked `src-tauri/engines.lock`-skeleton + cage-glob tail, then its check-off, per its own note) → this box's Loop half (`scripts/fetch-engine-assets`) → this box's owner-acked tail (the workflow wiring + the `g24-fetch-engine-assets.py` canary + the cage ruling above), batched with P4.28.1's workflow tail — P4.28.1's own Loop half (the harness scripts + the configure-flag manifest seam, per its note) precedes that shared tail.
  > **[DECIDED — owner adjudication 2026-09-01, the (A′) ruling on the P4.28 escalation]** the Loop half hard-stopped on a genuine gap rather than an implementation slip: the P4.56.1 schema carried neither a hash the populate path could verify a DOWNLOAD against (`sha256` is of the STAGED bytes per `RowKind::StagedArtifact`, i.e. of a file INSIDE an archive asset) nor a grouping token (`ffmpeg`/`ffprobe`/`libmp3lame.so` are three rows and ONE download, while `stage-engines` addresses `ffprobe` as a MEMBER inside the `ffmpeg` entry). Ruling: extend the schema by **three** fields with a mode split — **`cache_engine`** + **`cache_version`** mode-INDEPENDENT (defaults `id`/`version`; P4.28.1's CI compile populates the SAME key), and **`asset_sha256`** mode-CONDITIONAL, required-iff-prebuilt, the twin of `from_source.tarball_sha256`, `DuplicateSha256`-exempt, equal to `sha256` in the bare-download case. Invariant: **(cache_engine, triple) ↦ exactly one (cache_version, upstream_url, asset_sha256)** [Mode-scoped 2026-09-01 by the (A′) follow-up on P4.28.1: the `(upstream_url, asset_sha256)` half holds for a PREBUILT group only — a from-source group is a COMPILE and may consume several signed tarballs; one `cache_version` and one `acquisition` per group stay mode-independent] — explicitly NOT version-keyed, since `libmp3lame` 3.100 inside the `ffmpeg-7.1` entry would otherwise fire the populate path's own ambiguity hard-fail at a correct manifest. Riding the same act: the corroboration-URL **plural** + the `prebuilt_corroboration` submode (`mirrors` needs ≥ 2 URLs — §3.8's bare-hash case — `signed-repo` needs 1), the sibling source-anchor check re-keyed onto the GROUP token [Re-keyed AGAIN 2026-09-01 by the (A′) follow-up on P4.28.1: onto the SOURCE URL, not the group — a from-source group spans several tarballs, so group keying would report a correct compile as anchor drift; "same source ⇒ same anchors" is the one law that holds in both modes], the **`STAGED_ENGINES` ↔ manifest binder** in `stage-engines` (fail-open while the manifest is empty; its §6.1.3 tally bump in `g24-stage-engines.py` is the pre-declared owner-acked L(-1) tail), the container sweeps (§3.7.2 item 1, §3.4.4a's "one row = one sha256", `lock.rs`'s row law, P4.56.1's note above), the P4.56.3 allow-list keyed on `cache_engine`, the §6.1.3 `[OPEN]` closed onto `asset_sha256`, a per-hop **https + allow-listed** redirect policy in the fetch script, and §3.8-(i) narrowed literal→normative to **mirrors-only** (two build PROVIDERS make two different binaries, so their hashes cannot corroborate each other). All Loop-editable; the committed L(-1) skeleton is untouched. **Riding the owner-acked tail** (L(-1), so the Loop left them and names them here instead): `src-tauri/engines.lock`'s header still says `corroboration URL` SINGULAR where the schema now requires a plural whose `mirrors` submode needs ≥ 2, carries pre-(A′) text in THREE paragraphs, each named as a unit because a per-clause hand-off has already lost clauses: **(a) the ROW-LAW paragraph**, whose stale clauses are the unscoped "one row = one sha256", "the ONLY plural field is `triples`" (now false — `corroboration_urls` is a second one), "Every field is platform-pure and scalar" (which §3.4.4a now qualifies: `from_source` is a sub-table of scalars) and "if a field forks across triples, the row splits" (now scoped to ARTIFACT-identifying fields, since the cache-group fields are held EQUAL across a group instead); **(b) the CONSUMERS paragraph below it**, which still spells the §6.1.3 key `<engine>-<version>-<triple>` — the pre-(A′) ROW key, now `<cache_engine>-<cache_version>-<triple>` at every Loop-editable site, so this header is the last place in the repo contradicting `EngineRow::cache_key`; and **(c) the L(-1)/pin-provenance paragraph**, whose §3.7.2/§6.3.4 clause is where the SINGULAR "corroboration URL recorded on the row" actually lives — named separately because an owner re-blessing only (a) and (b) would leave standing the one clause whose consequence this note spells out; and the two caged security docs still carry the pre-narrowing wording, each in its own words: `docs/security/build-gates.md` (G37) says "≥ 2 independent **mirrors/providers**", while `docs/security/security-concept.md` (T3) already says "≥ 2 independent mirrors" and is stale only in its FFmpeg clause, "or a ≥ 2-provider cross-check" — quoted separately because a grep for one string finds only one of them. An owner following that header would record ONE corroboration URL for a `mirrors` pin and be rejected by the validator, so the re-bless rides the same act.     > invariant holds `(cache_engine, triple)` to one `(cache_version, upstream_url, asset_sha256)`.

    > `upstream_url`s (ffmpeg.org's and lame's) and two different `from_source` anchors, which the
    > invariant and the group-keyed sibling-anchor check REJECTED as shipped at 17808aa. Nothing
    > was red at the time (no from-source group existed), and the (A′) ruling's text had been
    > implemented as written rather than silently narrowed — which is why the narrowing came here,
    > as an owner act, rather than as a Loop reconcile. The review proposed the narrowing — scope the group
    > `upstream_url`/`asset_sha256` equality and the group-keyed anchor comparison to
    > `acquisition = "prebuilt"` rows, since a compiled entry has no shared download to identify

    > **THE RULING:** the proposed narrowing is ADOPTED, with one precision. Mode-INDEPENDENT and
    > kept: `(cache_engine, triple) ↦ exactly one cache_version` AND `↦ exactly one acquisition`
    > (an entry is EITHER one download OR one compile output — §3.8 decides acquisition per engine
    > per platform, so a mixed-mode group is a contradiction in terms and the validator rejects
    > it). Scoped to `acquisition = "prebuilt"`: the group equality of `(upstream_url,
    > asset_sha256)` — one entry is one download. For from-source groups BOTH are replaced by the
    > URL-KEYED anchor consistency: rows carrying the SAME `upstream_url` MUST carry identical
    > `from_source` anchors (ffmpeg + ffprobe out of one signed tarball stay consistency-checked),
    > while rows with different `upstream_url`s carry their own per-component anchors by
    > construction (libmp3lame's LAME tarball beside ffmpeg's) — the URL-keyed rule holds globally
    > across modes, so "same source ⇒ same anchors" is one law, not two. Owner may overturn.

    > **[Dual review, r1 — both reviewers NOGO, three P0s; the findings and the re-cut they
    > forced are recorded because the SHAPE of the miss is the reusable lesson]:** the first cut
    > tested each half only against fixtures the OTHER half could not produce, so 194 green legs
    > sat on top of a `--source` path that published an UNPACKED tree (the compile step needs raw
    > archives — a signature is over the archive as shipped) and keyed every source of a group
    > under ONE entry name, where the second `os.replace` lost to the first and was REPORTED as a
    > benign concurrent race at exit 0. One of the eight source legs asserted that collision as
    > the contract. The re-cut makes the source plan per-GROUP with N raw sources inside it,
    > fetches each `signature_url` beside its tarball, pairs rows to files by DECLARED name
    > instead of `sorted()` order, filters on `triples`, and binds the two halves with an
    > exact-contents assertion; the leg both reviewers named as the one that would have caught all
    > three — `fetch --source` and `compile_entry` against ONE cache — now exists and drives the
    > real sibling. 40 mutations were run over the result; all 40 red, including the two that
    > exposed arm-blind legs of my own (the argv leg passed with `--source` DROPPED, because both
    > modes exit 0 under `--check` — it now asserts the REPORT; and `filter="data"` had no
    > discriminating case until the sibling's device-node leg was ported).
    > **[Dual review, r2 — both reviewers NOGO again on the re-cut; recorded because the second
    > round found a DIFFERENT class than the first]:** r1's misses were two halves never run
    > together; r2's were invariants each half states about itself that nothing exercised.
    > **(1)** the two halves still contradicted each other on the shape §3.8's URL-keyed anchor
    > rule exists FOR: `fetch --source` deduped rows sharing one tarball (`ffmpeg` + `ffprobe` out
    > of one signed archive — `lock.rs`'s own worked example) while `plan_compiles` iterated rows
    > and refused the same manifest as a filename collision. Planning is now keyed on the SOURCE:
    > one tarball is one build that records every row id it produces, and exactly one of those rows
    > carries the line configuring it. **(2)** the from-source filename derivation had re-spelled
    > `assert_confined_member`'s grammar and lost its Windows drive-relative rule, so a
    > `.../D:evil.tar.gz` tail escaped the cache root with UNVERIFIED signature bytes at exit 0 —
    > closed at the class, not the instance: within each script every URL-derived path component
    > now goes through that script's ONE confinement grammar, so a rule added there protects every
    > caller in it. The two scripts still carry a copy each — they are independent stdlib tools
    > with no shared module — and what binds them is `resolve_inputs`' exact-contents assertion,
    > which fails loudly if either side ever derives a different name. **(3)** an empty prefix published as a permanent cache
    > hit, and the seam refused only `--prefix=` while `DESTDIR=`/`-DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX`/an
    > absolute `--libdir` redirected the install out of the staging tree just as effectively.
    > **(4)** the harness hardcoded autotools, which four of the five engines its own seam names
    > cannot use — FFmpeg ships a handwritten `configure` with no `configure.ac` for `autoreconf`
    > to work from, libvips is Meson, x265 and poppler are CMake — so the build system is now
    > DECLARED per source (`<engine>.configure.system`) off a table the driver set derives from.
    > **(5)** a Windows/POSIX split verified empirically during the review: `os.replace(dir →
    > existing FILE)` raises `ENOTDIR` on POSIX but SUCCEEDS on Windows, so both publish paths'
    > `if not destination.is_dir(): raise` was unreachable on Windows and a stray file at an entry
    > path was silently clobbered; both now refuse a non-directory before the move, identically on
    > every platform. **Two of my own legs were unfalsifiable** — `tuple(sorted(_VERIFY_ARGV)) ==
    > VERIFICATION_TOOLS` compares an expression to its own definition, and I wrote a second one
    > of exactly that shape for the build-system table in the round that found the first; both are
    > replaced by legs that DRIVE every table entry.
    > **[Dual review, r3 — both reviewers NOGO; the class was my own two r2 fixes never meeting]:**
    > **(1)** the round that added Meson and CMake did not extend the install-redirect guard the
    > same round's sibling finding had written, so `-Dprefix=` and `-DCMAKE_INSTALL_LIBDIR=` walked
    > past a guard that knew only `--libdir=` — and CMake's last-`-D`-wins makes the second an
    > OVERRIDE, not the ambiguity the guard's own message claims. It now normalises the option NAME
    > across all four spellings before looking anything up, and refuses the split two-token form
    > whose value a per-token guard cannot see. **(2)** a multi-source group could not name its own
    > shared prefix — it is a `mkdtemp` path invented at run time, so `--extra-ldflags=-L<prefix>/lib`
    > was unspellable and P6.1.1 (FFmpeg + libmp3lame), the exact shape the (A′) ruling and the r2
    > re-cut exist for, could not have been built at all. A `{prefix}` placeholder is substituted at
    > build time, any other placeholder is refused rather than passed through, and manifest order is
    > documented as load-bearing. **(3)** `<engine>.configure.source_dir`: x265 keeps its
    > `CMakeLists.txt` in `source/`, so the build root is not always the tree root.
    > **(4)** `produces` was written and pinned by two legs but read by nothing, so a two-row build
    > still reported one row; `--check` counted cache hits as work still to do. **(5)** the fetch
    > docstring claimed `compile-engine-asset` "shells out to this one" — it does not, and the
    > `.github/**` tail is authored from these docstrings, so an owner following it would have wired
    > one step where two are needed.
    > **[Dual review, r4 — both reviewers NOGO; the class was a VALUE never checked as a path]:**
    > the r3 install-redirect guard checked the option NAME across four spellings but the VALUE
    > only for "is it absolute", which was three holes at once — CMake's own typed spelling
    > (`-DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX:PATH=`, the one `CMakeCache.txt` uses), a plain relative
    > `--libdir=../../../../etc/evil`, and `--libdir={prefix}/../../escape` whose escape only
    > appears AFTER substitution. All three are closed by one line: the value now goes through
    > `_assert_confined`, the same grammar every other path-shaped thing in the script uses, so
    > absolute / `..`-traversing / drive-relative / interior-NUL land on one rule and `{prefix}`
    > survives as a plain first segment because it anchors AT the prefix. Also: the prefix reached
    > POSIX build tooling in NATIVE spelling (`--prefix=C:\` with backslashes into a `./configure`
    > shell word) and the leg meant to cover it built its expectation from the implementation's own
    > `str(prefix)`, so it could not fail on Windows — `as_posix()` at all four sites, and the leg
    > now asserts the PROPERTY. `${ORIGIN}` was over-refused as an unknown placeholder when it is

- [x] **P4.29** [BUILD] Build the macOS universal-sidecar `lipo -create` step + the per-sidecar `lipo -info` fat-Mach-O assertion · §6.1.3 §3.4.5 · G30 G37
  needs: P4.27, P4.28
  > the §6.1.3 macOS-leg requirement: `stage-engines` (NOT Tauri — Tauri does not lipo sidecars) `lipo -create`s both per-arch slices into one `<name>-universal-apple-darwin` fat binary for the externalBin slot before `tauri build`; the dual-arch sourcing (both `aarch64`+`x86_64` slices from the cache); the per-sidecar `lipo -info` assertion (both slices present, the G30 fat-Mach-O check) so a single-arch sidecar fails the leg, never ships. The missing-`x86_64`-slice from-source/cross-Rosetta fallback (§6.1.3's "Dual-arch sourcing fallback `[DECIDED]`" bullet) is the sub-box P4.29.1 below. **macOS ordering with the dylib relocation (P4.30): `lipo` runs FIRST, then the install_name relocation** — `lipo -create` re-emits the Mach-O (rewriting load commands), so an install_name rewrite applied before it would be clobbered; the P4.30 macOS relocation leg therefore consumes the lipo'd fat binary, never the per-arch slice.
    > **[Loop share DELIVERED 2026-09-03]** `scripts/stage-engines` accepts `universal-apple-darwin` (the P4.27 skeleton refused it, naming this box): `_slice_entries` sources BOTH per-arch entries per row, `merge_universal_binary` merges them behind an injected runner seam so the 124-leg `--selftest` needs no `lipo` and no macOS, and `assert_universal_fat` refuses the result unless it is a fat Mach-O carrying BOTH slices — the G30 case, asserted at the step that PRODUCED the file rather than only at the later gate sweep. The fat check is a portable stdlib header parse, not `lipo -info`, for the same reason `scripts/check-build-matrix` parses it (P0.4.10): `lipo` is macOS-only and a check that cannot run is not a check. **Resource trees are merged too**, not just sidecars — §6.1.3 says two slices per engine and P10.10 says `stage-engines` reads both slices "per sidecar/lib": `merge_universal_tree` requires both trees to carry the same paths, lipos each Mach-O pair, copies byte-identical data, preserves an agreeing symlink, and REFUSES anything else (a differing non-Mach-O has no merge rule, and guessing one is how a universal bundle ships one architecture's data). A one-slice cache is a refusal naming the missing slice and P4.29.1, never a silent half-universal artifact. Mutation posture after EIGHT review rounds: 60 of 68 mutants die. The r3 note claimed its survivors were "the host-gated set" and **r4 falsified that claim** — several had no leg at all — so the posture is stated per survivor, with the mechanism that makes each inert HERE. Three are exec-bit assertions: Windows carries no `S_IXUSR` on a file without an executable extension, so they read `… if os.name != "nt" else True` and are live on POSIX by construction, the assertion BEING the mode check. Five are link-shaped: the four UNIVERSAL legs this host records as `(skipped - host cannot create symlinks)` — seven leg names carry that suffix in all, the other three being the per-arch staging legs this box did not touch — which also carry the two type-mismatch message needles, and the tree-walk leg, which proves the JUNCTION half here because the host falls back to one. None of the eight is left on that argument alone — all five link-shaped ones were driven under a FAITHFUL POSIX simulation (a junction with `is_junction`/`is_symlink`/`readlink` patched to the three predicates that differ), where each baseline passes and each mutant fails. **r5 killed three mutants that no leg had reached before it, by making one leg portable rather than declaring it POSIX-only:** the `finalize` ORDERING had no leg on ANY platform — every ordering leaves an identical final file, so a leg reading the finished mode cannot see it — and it is now observed INSIDE the rename, from a read-only source rather than an exec bit, because Windows honours the write bit. The three it newly kills are the merge-path ordering swap, the tree member's mode re-applied after the merge returns, and the dropped tree-member `finalize` hook. **The two survivors r3 named are closed**, not carried: `assert_no_escaping_links` on the universal TREE path has a leg driving BOTH slices (the SECOND deliberately — a guard placed on the primary alone would pass every leg written against the first), and `merge_universal_tree` no longer returns at the path-set diff, so `--check` reaches every member verdict the real run reaches, gating on `write` only the statements that CREATE something. A dry run greener than the wet one is the one thing a check mode must never be; the single verdict it still cannot reach is `lipo`'s own, which needs the tool. **A third r3 P1 did not survive verification:** `rglob` was reported to follow directory symlinks on 3.12, which the installed stdlib refutes — `_iterate_directories` delegates to `Path.walk(follow_symlinks=False)`, so a dir symlink is classified as a file and never entered. The reliance was real even though the bug was not, and it is now SPELLED rather than inherited from an unstated default: `relatives` walks explicitly, preserving a symlink and descending a junction — exactly `copytree(symlinks=True)`, which is what the per-arch path does — with a leg that proves whichever half the host can express. The asymmetry that made this worth pinning was measured, not read: `rglob` DOES descend a Windows junction. **A fourth r3 item is adjudicated out of scope:** sidecar destinations have no `clear_*` analogue, but a sidecar is republished whole by `os.replace` rather than merged into, and Tauri bundles the sidecars `externalBin` ENUMERATES rather than the directory's contents, so a row-removal residue is neither merged nor shipped — unlike the resource-tree case that motivated the clear. It is also a property of BOTH staging paths, not something this box introduced. r3 also closed an unasserted claim of my own: the already-universal sidecar copy was called atomic with nothing proving it (a residue check cannot tell the two shapes apart, since neither leaves residue), so a leg now fails the copy AFTER the bytes land and asserts the previous sidecar survives intact. **r4 then found that leg still could not see a COPIED publish** — the injected failure fires mid-copy and never reaches the rename — so a second leg fails AT the publish instead: two injection points, because one cannot observe both steps. **r4's two blocking findings were both mine to answer.** Both reviewers independently reported the same one: the `--check`-depth class was closed for the tree and left open in its SIBLING, the sidecar branch, whose whole body — including the pure fatness verdict on restored cache bytes — sat under `if write:`. The second: a leg I added in r2 planted its directory symlink by pointing it at its own parent, which `pathlib` writes as an ABSOLUTE link — refused by `assert_no_escaping_links` on an arm of its own before the merge is ever entered, so the leg was green here ONLY because this host skips it and would have recorded a FAIL on every POSIX runner, reddening Lane-A on two of three OS.     > **[Loop share DELIVERED 2026-09-05]** the whole mechanism lands in `scripts/compile-engine-asset` (un-caged), one step UPSTREAM of `stage-engines`, which keeps reading the cache and never compiles — so the §6.1.3 one-socket-at-package-time claim and P4.29's one-slice refusal both stand unchanged. **The hole it closes was live:** `--target` was a row FILTER and a cache-key SUFFIX and nothing else (`CompilePlan.triple` was written and never read), so `--target x86_64-apple-darwin` on an arm64 mac exited 0 and published a NATIVE arm64 tree under the x86_64 key — `lipo -create` would then meet two arm64 slices and report a duplicate architecture naming neither the row nor the build. That is the silent single-arch class **G30** exists for, one step upstream of where G30 can see it. **Five parts:** (1) `resolve_build(host, target)` resolves the pair against a DECLARED `_CROSS_BUILDS` table — §6.1.3 declares exactly one pair, `aarch64-apple-darwin → x86_64-apple-darwin` — and REFUSES an undeclared mismatch (exit 2) rather than guessing native; it is resolved FIRST in `run()`, before either manifest is read, so `--check` catches an impossible target whatever the manifest says (asserted with BOTH manifest paths absent, or "missing engine manifest" would win and the ordering leg would pass vacuously). (2) Per-build-system cross argv, each in that system's own canonical spelling (`autotools` `--host`/`--build` + the `CC=`/`CXX=` argument assignments; `configure` = FFmpeg's handwritten dialect, `--enable-cross-compile --arch --target-os --cc`, which is why that system name exists at all; `cmake` `CMAKE_OSX_ARCHITECTURES` + the generic toolchain triple + `CMAKE_CROSSCOMPILING_EMULATOR`; `meson` a GENERATED cross file). It is APPENDED, so the native argv is a strict PREFIX of the cross argv — the box's "same curated configure flags as the arm64 slice" clause, stated as a property a leg can check rather than as prose. `_cross_argv` REFUSES a build system with no cross spelling, and a leg pins `set(_CROSS_ARGV) == set(BUILD_SYSTEMS)`, so a widened `BUILD_SYSTEMS` cannot arrive cross-blind. (3) The Meson cross file is generated BY the script into the build tree, never declared: `--cross-file` is one of the `_IMPORTED_SETTINGS_OPTIONS` a manifest may never carry, and a leg re-asserts that a DECLARED `--cross-file` is still refused — generating one here does not widen the guard. (4) Rosetta 2 is probed once per ENTRY — after that entry's signatures verify, before its first build step, never above the loop, since a run whose entries are all cache hits must not demand Rosetta — (`arch -x86_64 /usr/bin/true` through the injected runner), because macOS runs a thin x86_64 binary transparently but a build that executes a tool it just built needs Rosetta, and without the probe that surfaces mid-build as an opaque exec error; an absent `arch` is a structural refusal, not a traceback. No build STEP is wrapped in the emulator — the wrapper is what Meson's `exe_wrapper` and CMake's `CMAKE_CROSSCOMPILING_EMULATOR` are handed. (5) `assert_target_arch` walks the finished prefix and requires every Mach-O to carry EXACTLY the entry's own CPU type — a foreign arch is a wrong build and an already-FAT member cannot be merged either (`lipo` refuses a duplicate architecture), so "carries the target" is not enough. It runs on BOTH darwin triples, native and cross, which is what makes the merge of the two slices meaningful. The header constants are frozen to the **G30** gate's own copies (read, not imported — the gate is L(-1), its VALUE is not), with a shape rule asserting the freeze table covers every such constant this module defines, and one fat fixture is driven through BOTH this parser and `stage-engines.fat_slice_cputypes` so producer and consumer cannot answer one question two ways. **No `engines.lock` shape is needed:** `acquisition` is a scalar on a triple-keyed row and the cache-group invariant is keyed `(cache_engine, triple)` (`lock.rs`), so "x86_64 slice from source while the arm64 sibling stays prebuilt" is already writable and already fixture-tested — the fallback is that declaration, not a new conditional mode. **Every leg is hermetic:** the injected runner plus an explicitly-passed host drive the macOS mechanism — including a full cross build THROUGH `run()`, not merely around it at the `compile_entry` level, which the dual review's round-2 mutation pass proved was the difference between a claim and a covered seam (dropping `run()`'s own `cross=cross` threading survived the whole suite until that leg landed) — on a Linux or Windows runner with no compiler, no `arch`, no `lipo` and no mac — required, because the caged canary tolerates ZERO skipped legs on POSIX, and the skip inventory is unchanged (still exactly the pinned symlink trio). Tallies re-derived from `--selftest` on the committed tree, never carried: `compile-engine-asset` 208 → **257**, `stage-engines` 124 → **126**. Mutation posture: 18 hand-written mutants over the new guards across BOTH tools, **18 killed, no survivor**. The last of them was the `">I"`→`">i"` fat-header revert, which every fixture of REAL cputypes reads as equivalent (all below 2^31) — rather than leave it declared equivalent, the producer/consumer cross-check now drives a fixture carrying a high-bit cputype, which turns the disagreement into an observable one and the mutant into a kill. It is measured CROSS-TOOL, since the leg lives in `compile-engine-asset` and loads the real sibling: the sibling is mutated in place, `compile-engine-asset --selftest` is run against it, and the file is restored and hash-verified byte-identical — measured with an OUT-OF-TREE harness (a session artifact in the scratchpad, deliberately not committed: the repo's mutation plane is G15's Rust ratchet, and a second permanent harness for one Python tool would be a structure this box has no mandate to add), so the number is a reported measurement, not a re-runnable in-tree artifact. One earlier survivor (raising the fat arch-count bound) was a VACUOUS leg of mine, answered by the truncation check rather than by the bound, and is closed by a fixture that carries its whole over-length table plus a second leg for the truncation case itself. `run()`'s `host` is a REQUIRED keyword, not a defaulted one, so a caller cannot reach the refusal path by forgetting it.

    > **[Loop share DELIVERED 2026-09-05]** the mechanism lands in `scripts/stage-engines` as a post-staging TAIL, reached from BOTH branches of `stage_all` — the per-arch loop and the universal one — because the four publish paths that carry no `finalize` hook would otherwise be unreachable by whichever rule the rewrite hung off. **Data, then dispatch:** a `Relocation` row declares `dest`/`member` (the binding key into a staged row), `set_id`, the `(absolute install name, bundled basename)` `references` pairs, and `origin`; `RELOCATIONS` ships EMPTY exactly as `STAGED_ENGINES` does, since a rewrite is inseparable from the component set it rewrites — P6.1.2 is the first filler. `_RELOCATORS` maps every §3.4.5 v1 triple PLUS `universal-apple-darwin` to its argv builder and `relocator_for` REFUSES anything else rather than defaulting to a no-op, so a widened triple set cannot arrive silently unrelocated; a leg pins that coverage in both directions, and a second pins the read-back table against it. The three legs are the box's three sub-boxes: macOS emits `-id @loader_path/<final name>` and `-change <abs> @loader_path/<lib>` in ONE `install_name_tool` invocation (the tool applies them together, so a half-rewritten Mach-O is not a reachable state); Linux emits `patchelf --set-rpath '$ORIGIN'` as one literal argv token, which is the only reason the `$` survives to the tool at all under `shell=False`; Windows returns no argv and is DECLARED in the table, which is what makes "nothing to do here" distinguishable from "nobody considered it". **The rewrite is atomic**, like every other publish in the file: it runs against a `.{name}.relocating` sibling and lands with one `os.replace`, so a failing tool leaves the staged binary byte-identical — rewriting the published file directly would re-open precisely the torn-publish window the P4.29 legs pin shut — and the tool is told the FINAL basename explicitly, so the temp name can never leak into an install name. **What it asserts, and what it refuses to claim:** the tool succeeded, the result is a non-empty file of the SAME container format (a rewrite edits load commands, never the magic), and — where the toolchain can answer precisely — the result is READ BACK (`patchelf --print-rpath` covers the whole Linux mechanism; `otool -D` covers the macOS `-id` half). `expected_load_paths` yields a `(tool, expected, forbidden)` triple per DECLARED operation, so no half is left unchecked; what it does NOT do is enumerate load commands or assert CLOSURE, which is **G37b**'s and would be this box claiming a gate the plan gives to another. All five have legs now. The §6.1.3 macOS clause also said the two operations travel "in one invocation" in a way that read as two different FILES in one call, which is impossible; the rule is per FILE. **Round 3 found the class a THIRD time and two more of my own claims.** `stage_all` gained three new lines; two got wiring legs and the BINDER call did not — deleting `assert_relocations_bound` left all 169 legs green while a typo'd `dest` fell through `relocate_all`'s absent-row arm and silently skipped the rewrite, the exact failure this box exists to catch. The runner default was unpinned the same way: every leg passed `runner=` explicitly, so the one production path reaching `run_tool` for the tail was untested. Both now have legs. Also: `-change` had landed in CODE while both doc sites still said it was deliberately unread (§6.1.3 named only `otool -D`, and the note still referenced `expected_load_path` singular, which no longer exists) — the enumeration-not-rule shape this commit fixes one screen away for `run_tool`; both are now stated as a rule. `relocate_all` collapsed several rows sharing one RESOURCE_TREE dest last-wins, so an absent engine could hide a destination another engine DID stage — `assert_relocations_bound` used set semantics and `relocate_all` did not, and the two disagreed about what a dest is. And `RowKind::StagedArtifact`'s summary still opened with the exact ambiguous words its own new body exists to remove. Tally 126 → **182**, re-derived from `--selftest` on the committed tree; the skip inventory is unchanged (the guards that would need a real symlink are driven through a stub, so no new skip name appears on a host without the privilege). **The dual review added five things, four of them narrowings of claims I had made too widely.** (i) `install_name_tool -change` matches its old path LITERALLY and exits 0 on no match — so the dependent binary, the one case with no `-id` to read back, was exactly where the silent-no-op class was still open. The read-back now covers EVERY declared operation, `-change` pairs through `otool -L`, over data the row already carries; that is not G37b arriving early, because G37b asserts CLOSURE over every load command including `LC_RPATH` and this asserts only that the declared pairs were applied.
