  needs: P4.27
  > the §3.3.1 Tauri config: `bundle.externalBin` listing the sidecars (`binaries/ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `soffice`, `pdftotext`, `pandoc`, `convertia-imgworker`) and `bundle.resources` mapping the LibreOffice tree / image stack / fonts / `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; no `updater`/`createUpdaterArtifacts` block (the G47 structural lint, P0.3.2, enforces the absence). Generic wiring; per-engine entries filled as P5–P7 stage each.
  > **[Loop share DELIVERED 2026-09-06]** the box's own closing clause decides its shape: the three engine-tree keys and **five of the six** sidecar entries are **per-engine fills**, so they arrive with their engines in P5–P7, exactly as `STAGED_ENGINES` (P4.27) and `RELOCATIONS` (P4.30) ship empty; the sixth, `binaries/convertia-imgworker`, arrives at **P4.34** in this phase and is not an engine at all (see the escape-hatch paragraph below). That is not a scoping convenience — it is **MEASURED**: `tauri-build` 2.6.3's build script copies sidecars AND resources at **COMPILE** time (`copy_binaries` / `copy_resources`, its `lib.rs` `bundle.external_bin` / `BundleResources` arms), and `copy_file`/`resource_from_path` hard-fail on a source that is not there — so `--no-bundle` does **not** hide a declared-but-unstaged entry, and declaring the six sidecars against an empty `src-tauri/binaries/` would red the G30 compile-sanity leg on all three OS. What the box therefore lands is the **wiring in the two senses that are real today**. **(1) The one map entry that is NOT a per-engine fill:** `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`, the repo-ROOT file P1.48 authored and P2.98 `include_str!`s. §3.3.1's literal spelled it un-prefixed, but a `bundle.resources` SOURCE path resolves against the config's OWN directory (`src-tauri/`), so the literal named `src-tauri/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` — a file that does not exist — and `copy_resources` would hard-fail on it at compile time. Reconciled **literal→normative** to `../THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` (the same reconcile §3.3.1's own 2026-07-22 note made on the other three keys; the bundle-internal target stays `""`, Tauri's documented "keep the file name, at the resource root" map case) and **verified empirically rather than by reading**: `cargo check -p convertia-core` executes the build script and lands `target/debug/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`. **(2) The BINDER**, which is the substance. Both config keys have existed EMPTY since the P1 scaffold, so a box that only re-stated them would be a no-op; and **nothing** tied `STAGED_ENGINES` to `tauri.conf.json` — G47 asserts only the config's negative properties (no updater, no `fileAssociations`, the macOS floor) and never the engine set. G47 cannot be extended from here (`scripts/check-*` is caged), so the binder lands in the un-caged `scripts/stage-engines`, beside the `engines.lock` binder it mirrors. **Both directions, because they fail differently:** the FORWARD miss (a staged row no entry declares) is the **silent** one — Tauri packages only what it is told about, the build is green, and the shipped app faults at runtime as a missing engine (§7.2.3); the REVERSE miss does reach `tauri-build`, but one compile later and phrased as a missing FILE rather than as two declarations having drifted apart. **Shape, never a name list:** both prefixes are asserted structurally (`binaries/<name>` per §3.3.2 step 2, `resources/<subdir>/` per step 3), because a member-set comparison passes a bare `"ffmpeg"` entry (Tauri would resolve `src-tauri/ffmpeg-<triple>`, a path nothing writes) and drops a `resources/libreofice/` typo out of the comparison entirely instead of failing it — the enumeration-guards-cannot-close-a-class shape, with each hole given its own leg. The two prefixes are **DERIVED** from `BINARIES_REL`/`RESOURCES_REL` rather than restated, so a re-home of the staging destinations moves both sides at once. Map entries outside `resources/` are the non-engine bundled resources (§3.3.1's licences text, §3.3.2 step 4's per-build SBOM + NOTICE) and are explicitly not the staging step's to claim. **The wiring is pinned from the first commit**, not after a review measured it: the class this box's two predecessors hit four times running is a guard nothing calls, so the binder is reached from `stage_all` **and** — because `main()` returns before `stage_all` while `STAGED_ENGINES` is empty — from that early return too, which makes the reverse direction live TODAY rather than arming with the first P5 row. Each seam carries its own leg: `main()` refuses a config-unbound row set with the manifest left fail-OPEN (so only the bundle binder can decide that run), and the no-rows path refuses an `externalBin` entry nothing stages while `STAGED_ENGINES` is genuinely empty, beside its fail-open twin so the leg cannot be passing for the wrong reason. **The reverse direction needed a DECLARED escape hatch, and the round-1 review is what found it.** The shape rule alone would have met P4.34 with a guard that cannot be satisfied: §3.3.1's sixth `externalBin` entry, `binaries/convertia-imgworker`, is the first-party crate `crates/imgworker` (§3.5.5/§0.7) compiled by cargo, not an asset restored from the §6.1.3 cache — and it can never carry a `STAGED_ENGINES` row, because a row binds to an `engines.lock` entry (`assert_rows_bound_to_manifest`) and that manifest is the **third-party inventory**: every `EngineRow` carries an `upstream_url` and a `source_ref` naming an upstream tag/commit, which a first-party build product has none of (verified in `src-tauri/src/engines/lock.rs`, not taken from the reviewer's word). P4.34's only exits would have been a fake manifest row or weakening a landed guard — the precise trap the box's own docstring cites, whose prescription is shape rule **+ declared escape hatch**. So the hatch lands HERE, as the NAMED `BUILT_SIDECARS` set (the sidecar-side analogue of the resources side's non-`resources/` carve-out): named rather than pattern-matched, so a typo cannot hide behind it, and a name that is BOTH exempt and staged by a row is itself reported — an exemption for sidecars this script does not produce must not be able to shadow a real drift. Three legs, one per property. **Mutation posture: 31 hand-written mutants over the new binder — 31 killed, zero survivors.** They cover both readers' fail-closed arms, all four row↔config directions, both shape rules, all three escape-hatch properties, the one-derivation tree rule, the misspelled-prefix arm in both its case and separator forms, the derived prefixes, the SCOPE catcher, and all THREE wiring seams (the `stage_all` call, `main()` passing `conf_path`, and the no-rows early return). The FIRST run left exactly one survivor, and it was a hole in MY leg rather than in the guard: dropping the prefix filter from the declared-entry set survived, because the shape leg hands it a correctly-prefixed entry ALONGSIDE the bare one, so the forward direction was satisfied either way and only the weaker of the two messages was lost. The leg that closes it drives a row whose ONLY entry is un-prefixed and asserts the row is reported undeclared as well — the diagnostic has to say what to WRITE, not only what is wrong. **Also taken from round 1, all pre-push:** the reverse resource check `rstrip`ped its key where the forward half compared exact strings, so `resources/libreoffice//` read as a claim on a row the forward half simultaneously called undeclared — the two halves disagreed about what a key IS, and they now read ONE derivation; a key that MEANS `resources/` without spelling it (a capital, a `\` separator — it resolves on a Windows authoring host and breaks on the Linux runner) escaped as an unrecognised non-engine entry and is now reported as the mistyped destination it is; a JSON `null`, which is how Tauri's own `Option` spells "absent", was refused as a type error; a duplicated array entry produced a duplicated message; the map-only narrowing of `bundle.resources` (Tauri also accepts a bare list) was undocumented; the two no-rows `main()` legs relied on the REAL row set still being empty and would have reddened at the first P5 row under names that no longer described them, so they now patch an explicitly empty set; §3.3.2 step 4 was quoted without its patent-disposition record; the SCOPE catcher's driven-comparison leg still used `4.31` as its example of an id NOT decided in this file; and P1.19's note still described the two config slots as "empty-but-declared". **Round 2 returned GO/GO and its four P3s were taken anyway**, since each was one line and the anchor is the cleanest solution, not the cheapest: the `claimed_trees` comment claimed ONE derivation while the forward half re-spelled the key twice more (three literals agreeing today is the same drift one commit away — they now all read `_tree_key`); the `_means_prefix` separator arm was pinned only as `!= []`, which does not say WHICH arm spoke, and now carries the needle like its case sibling; the exempted sidecar's residual backstop (`tauri-build`'s own compile-time `copy_binaries` failure) was left implicit at the one site that deliberately stops checking, so the exemption now states what still catches it; and the bare `resources` spelling — which would make Tauri walk the WHOLE staging tree into the resource root under the wrong internal paths — fell through unflagged, so `_means_prefix` matches the prefix without its trailing slash and then end-or-separator, with `resources-readme.md` pinned in the over-fire direction. **Two container texts moved in the same act:** the module docstring's SCOPE paragraph listed this box as a piece the file does NOT carry — P4.30's freshness catcher would have reddened the moment P4.31 is ticked, which is the catcher working — so P4.31 joins the landed clause with the distinction stated (the config lives in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`; what the file carries is the check that the two declarations cannot drift); and that catcher's own comment used P4.31 as its example of "a box that wires `tauri.conf.json` and never touches this file", which turned out false for the reason above — the tag-keyed form was right for a reason that outlived its illustration, and the comment now says so. **DoD item 2 forced spec authoring, not a reference:** a repo-wide grep found the two config keys named in §3.3.1/§6.1.3 and nowhere the RULE that the two sides must agree, so §6.1.3 gains the both-directions binding bullet (incl. the compile-time-copy fact that makes `--no-bundle` irrelevant here) and §3.3.1's map literal carries the `../` correction. Tally 182 → **214**.

Ruling: §7.2.4's naming duty wins, the catalog keeps ownership, §2.8.2's row takes §7.2.4's literal with `{engine name}` — landed as its own commit ahead of this box, with the code literal + its pin + a spec↔code bind. `QuarantinedByOs` thereby becomes a slotted kind on the Running→Failed path, and the rule for every slotted row that fails an item (`crate::orchestrator::project_outcome`) is that the arg is supplied at the row's own raise site — so **P4.46.1 MUST supply the `{engine name}` arg at its raise site** or `arg = ""` renders a blank-worded line. The same act closed a latent instance of that rule: `PlatformUnavailable`'s registry-miss raise site passed no `{platform}` arg — unreachable on the LIVE path today only because the C6 handler's source-side `resolve_slice_target` validation precedes the conductor, and armed the moment P5–P7 widen the slice; it now supplies `Platform::display_name()`, pinned by a rendered-text test and an execution test that drives `convert_item` into the registry miss. **(2)** §7.2.1's step-4 line states no failure verdict of its own (steps 2 and 3 each state theirs inline), and while the §7.2.1 `[DECIDED]` readiness-fault split does bound steps 3–5 to `EngineMissing`/`BundleDamaged`, it does not say WHICH of the two a step-4 permission failure takes — and the app-fault-vs-degrade fork is P4.46's. Ruling: an `ensure_executable` failure is an **app-level fault (§2.13) mapping to `EngineMissing`** (§2.8 "absent/**unrunnable** at startup" — a present-but-not-executable sidecar is unrunnable; a mode bit is not byte damage, so not `BundleDamaged`); this is the **pre-fork default**, and P4.46 still owns the later required-vs-degrade re-routing.
  > **[Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn] Absent-sidecar rule for step 4 (pre-fill).** The 2026-09-07 ruling covers a PRESENT sidecar that cannot be made executable. At fill time no sidecar is present (`externalBin: []`), every path the engine-program table resolves misses, and the helper returns `Err(NotFound)` for a missing file (pinned by P1.17's missing-file test). Mapping that `Err` to `EngineMissing` would fault every launch, invisibly to every gate. **Ruling: presence is step 3's finding (P4.42) and its routing P4.46's; step 4 is presence-agnostic.** `io::ErrorKind::NotFound` continues (a `log::debug!` naming the bare sidecar name, never a path, §7.5); any other `Err` is the `EngineMissing` app-fault. The skip lives at this call site, never inside the helper. The non-NotFound test arm uses an `exe_dir` that is a regular FILE (`NotADirectory`): a "present but unwritable" file cannot fail for its owner in a tempdir. The §7.2.1 step-4 verdict sentence the 92eeb2b ruling deferred to this box lands here with BOTH halves (DoD 2).

Without the release, `RunRegistry::finish` never runs and `converter_is_busy` stays true, wedging the §7.1.1 refuse-busy gate and the §7.3.2 close guard. "Emits nothing" means no wire `AppFault`, no `RunFinished` and no retained `RunResult` (§5.2 state 12 `[DECIDED]`: the run path carries no DTO). The frontend reaches state 12 through the watchdog below or an opaque C6/C7 rejection. The scratch the unwind leaves is the §2.6.3 sweep's, and the boundary survives the P4.86 concurrent re-cut. The mid-run copy spans §2.13.3 and §5.2 / §5.8, so the harmonized copy lands in both in the same commit. Test: a planted run-body panic leaves `has_active_run()` false, reaches a capturing `log::Log` redacted, and sends no `RunFinished`.
  > **Run liveness [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** No § sets a silence bound (§5.8, §5.2 row 12; §0.4.2 says "no heartbeat"). A healthy coarse item can stay silent for up to `SUBPROCESS_WALL_CLOCK_DEFAULT`, plus in-core stretches no §0.9 const bounds. A pure frontend timer can therefore false-fire a healthy run into state 12, which is forbidden. C8 cannot tell "still running" from "gone", because both answer `Err(InternalError)`. This box therefore adds the read-only command **C16 `get_run_liveness { runId } -> RunLiveness { Running | Finished | Unknown }`**, a pure read of `RunRegistry` + `RunResultStore`. C16 reads the run registry before the result store (`finish_run` retains the result before it finishes the run). On silence past a named interval the watchdog calls C16: `Running` keeps waiting, `Finished` re-fetches C8, and `Unknown`, a rejected call, or a call that does not settle within its own named bound fires `onRunFault`. No core heartbeat is added (§0.4.2 stands). Same-commit sync (DoD 2 / G68): the §0.4.1 C16 row, the §0.6 `RunLiveness` enum, §5.2 row 12 (a) and the §5.8 silence bullet (silence → C16 → `Unknown` / reject / unsettled → state 12), `src-tauri/ipc-commands.golden` (check 12), `xtask codegen` → `bindings.ts` + the `src/lib/ipc/commands.ts` wrapper, and the C-count literals and the `HANDLERS` table P4.67.1's sync list names; plan-lint check 9 admits C16 since 2026-09-15. Each C-count literal names the command set registered at this fill, since C16 may land before C15. Every threshold is a named constant tagged `[Build-Session-Entscheidung: P4.50]`. Tests: a quiet-but-alive run never reaches state 12; a panicking run task, a rejected C16 call and a C16 call that never settles (after its bound) each do; a `Finished` answer re-fetches C8.

An assertion taken earlier checks bytes that never ship, or runs a binary that cannot load its beside-the-exe libraries. (b) **`--check`:** the hook reads no staged byte and executes nothing. It still validates every byte-free contract: each declared assertion binds to a staged row, and its argv can be built (`relocate_all`'s rule that a check pass never validates less than the run). The declaration binder also runs on `main`'s no-rows branch (the P4.31 binder precedent), so it is live before the first P5 row. (c) **Slices the runner cannot run natively:** how an executing assertion treats the universal Mach-O's x86_64 slice (runnable via the P4.29.1 Rosetta probe) or a foreign `--target` is decided and tagged in this fill, or escalated. It is never downgraded to a structural-only check, because §6.1.3's execute-and-fail assertions are `[DECIDED]` against the shipped binary. (d) **SCOPE sentence, same commit:** remove `(P4.51)` from the `stage-engines` docstring's "Each excluded piece is its own box" sentence; a P4.51 decision tag with the id still listed reds the SCOPE-freshness leg. If P4.41 has already removed `(P4.41)`, the list empties and reds both the freshness and the non-vacuity legs (measured 2026-09-10). The second of P4.41/P4.51 to land therefore retires the sentence and moves the three SCOPE-freshness legs to their terminal state under the SAME leg names, with a `[Test-Change: <that box>]` rationale that passes G70. - [ ] **P4.52** [BUILD] Build the MIT-core LGPL shared-object-or-fail link assertion (static LGPL into the MIT core = build FAILURE) · §6.1.3 §3.6.1 · G38
  > the §6.1.3 [DECIDED] carve-out (i) assertion: no static LGPL link into the **MIT core** (§3.6.1 LGPL link build rule; mechanism note below). Scoped to the Rust core code object only. (§6.1.3 carve-out (ii) is its own box P4.76: a different code object, failing independently. Leg (iii) — FFmpeg-internal static LGPL — is aggregation that NEVER fails and carries no assertion body; it is a prose note in §3.6.1/`engines.lock`, not a build check.)
  > **[Mechanism — pre-fill audit 2026-09-10]** [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]: This box asserts the LINK KIND of every native library the MIT core's binary links. The crate-licence half is G18's `deny.toml` LGPL deny, which is blind here: every native library in the core's closure enters through an MIT-licensed `-sys` crate. **Data source:** the `cargo:rustc-link-lib=[KIND=]NAME` build-script directives of the packages in `convertia-core`'s NORMAL target closure (`cargo metadata --locked`). The `links` keys are not the source, because glib/gobject/gio/pango/cairo/gdk-pixbuf/soup3/javascriptcore-sys carry none. Host build-dependency outputs are excluded (vswhom-sys `static=vswhom` feeds a build script, not the binary). **Rule:** any `static=` (or `static:+whole-archive=`) native library not on a committed fail-closed permissive allow-list, seeded from a measurement on all three OS, FAILS the build. The allow-list lives at `scripts/core-static-links.toml`, auto-caged by the `scripts/*.toml` glob (the P4.56.3 `scripts/engine-sources.toml` pattern), and the Co-Pilot seeds it from the three-OS measurement in the same P4.81 act as the compile-sanity invocation. A dynamic link passes whether the shared object is bundled or OS-provided (§6.1.3 (i)), so no by-name GTK exclusion exists. **Hook site:** a post-core-build leg — compile-sanity after `pnpm tauri build --debug --no-bundle`, and the Lane-B release job after `tauri build`. It is not a `stage-engines` leg and has no `needs: P4.51`: the core does not exist at staging time, and `stage-engines --check` runs in a toolchain-less job. **Split:** the Loop builds the checker and unit-tests it against synthetic build-script `output` and metadata fixtures, homed outside the L(-1) cage (a `scripts/check-*` home is caged) with the home tagged at the site. Its compile-sanity CI invocation is a `.github/**` edit, a caged tail the P4.81 sweep closes. A neutered checker body is caught by a second P4.81 caged tail: the checker's own caged self-test file carries a planted positive from outside it (the P4.41 ruling's pattern, applied to a separate checker). The Lane-B release-job invocation is P10.9's (`needs: P4.52`), because `release.yml` is the P0.2.5 fail-closed skeleton and a step added now never runs. G37b scans staged engines only, so a binary-import cross-check of the core would be a new named leg, never a G37b ride-along.
- [ ] **P4.53** [BUILD] Build the libvips-no-copyleft-PDF-loader assertion (no poppler/mupdf/GPL/AGPL loader present) · §6.1.3 §3.1 §3.6.1 · G38
  needs: P4.34, P4.51
  > the §6.1.3 no-copyleft-PDF-loader `[DECIDED]` positive assertion (rationale §3.1/§3.6.1): fail the build if a `pdfload`/`poppler`/`mupdf` foreign loader is registered in the staged libvips. **Artifact + stage:** this is the **P4 proof-of-life** check on the **P4.34 imgworker's** statically-linked libvips (the first time libvips is in a built sidecar). P5.2 is the **distinct** stage-time check on the **newly-staged P5.1 libvips** before any P5 image engine builds against it — same property, different artifact at a different build stage (so each fact keeps one home, _format.md §8).
  > **[Mechanism — pre-fill audit 2026-09-10, verified against libvips v8.18.6; re-verify at the P4.89 pin]** A SYMBOL assertion, which §6.1.3's "loader/symbols" allows; a runtime enumeration would need the P4.35 FFI this box does not `needs:`. The main leg runs the pinned rustup `llvm-tools-preview` `llvm-nm` over the static libvips archive in the P4.34 cache entry, on all three OS. Any symbol matching `vips_foreign_load_pdf` (the poppler and pdfium loader classes, compiled only under `HAVE_POPPLER`/`HAVE_PDFIUM`), `poppler_` or `FPDF_` FAILS. `pdfload` is never a symbol needle: libvips always defines `vips_pdfload`/`vips_pdfload_buffer`/`vips_pdfload_source`, even with no PDF loader built. Those three wrappers are the proof that `llvm-nm` read libvips' foreign code instead: if they are absent the leg FAILS, never a silent pass. The same scan over the staged `convertia-imgworker-<triple>` runs only where the binary has a symbol table (ELF/Mach-O), with the same proof. On a symbol-less binary (a Windows PE keeps its symbols in the PDB) that leg is skip-with-reason, never absent = pass. **Configure cross-check:** the `libvips.configure.flags` P4.34 writes into `engine-configure.toml` must carry `-Dpoppler=disabled`, `-Dpdfium=disabled` and `-Dmodules=disabled`; a module build moves the poppler loader out of the archive the scan reads. libvips has no MuPDF loader; the `mupdf` word stays because §6.1.3 uses it.

(1) **Provenance leg:** EXPECTED `upstream_url == "https://github.com/lovell/libimagequant"` ∧ `version` matches `2.4.*` ∧ `source_ref == "ce5fdeb1ccd9db288950faa21fd09c42c0a59bbb"`, the commit the fork's only tag `v2.4.1` points to (a signed tag; the fork publishes no release). It is checked against EVERY per-triple `id = "libimagequant"` row of `src-tauri/engines.lock` (`EngineRow::source_ref`, `crate::engines::lock`). In `Cargo.lock`, any `imagequant`/`libimagequant` package with a registry source FAILS, because crates.io `imagequant` is the GPL-3.0-or-later ImageOptim upstream, never the fork. A git source passes only with the lovell URL and that commit. A fork bump changes the constant in the same commit as the row, and records the new tag's verified signature in the commit body. (2) **COPYRIGHT leg:** the file `COPYRIGHT` at the root of the pinned libimagequant source PASSES only if it contains "Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without modification" and contains neither "GNU GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE" nor "licensed under GPL v3 or later" (the upstream ImageOptim `COPYRIGHT` carries both GPL strings and no BSD sentence). The on-disk location is not fixed here: link-input cache entries hold headers and static libraries only, and P4.89 (b) decides whether libimagequant gets its own cache key or joins the libvips group prefix. The leg reads the pinned source's own `COPYRIGHT` at the licence-text location P4.89's acquisition records, never the `third-party-licenses/` copy (P4.57); a missing record FAILS, never a guessed path.

A symbol scan would pass silently in three cases: libheif's default plugin-form dav1d (`WITH_DAV1D_PLUGIN` defaults ON) leaves no `dav1d_*` symbol in the worker, an MSVC exe keeps its symbols in the PDB, and a stripped binary has none. The call lives in the worker's single G29 unsafe seat `crates/imgworker/src/ffi.rs` (hence `needs: P4.35`): `heif_get_decoder_descriptors` (returned sorted by priority) and `heif_decoder_descriptor_get_id_name`. It fails open only while no AV1 decoder is listed AND no dav1d/libaom row is staged, because the P4.34 stack stages no AV1 decoder and the slot turns fail-closed at P5.12; keying on the staged rows means a broken call never reads as "nothing staged". **Non-vacuity:** the same mode lists the HEVC decoders, and `libde265` from the P4.34 stack must appear, so an empty or broken enumeration FAILS. libheif stays a P4.34 prebuilt-mode input, so no `libheif.configure.flags` seam exists or is added here.

The TRIPLE is the key, not the OS — §3.4.5 gives macOS two of them and the universal artifact is `lipo`-merged from both — and the **OS level is the DERIVED view** the §3.4.4a parse→map flow projects onto `Platform`. Invariant: **(artifact, triple) ↦ exactly one row**, and **one row = one STAGED-BYTES `sha256`**; the moment an ARTIFACT-identifying field forks across triples, the row splits. [Scoped 2026-09-01 by the (A′) ruling on the P4.28 escalation: the §6.1.3 cache-GROUP fields (`cache_engine`/`cache_version`/`upstream_url`/`asset_sha256`) are deliberately NOT artifact-identifying — several rows ship out of ONE cache entry, so they are held EQUAL across a group rather than forcing it apart, and `asset_sha256` is duplicate-hash-EXEMPT because siblings of one archive carry it identically by construction. Mode-scoped 2026-09-01 by the (A′) follow-up on P4.28.1: the `upstream_url`/`asset_sha256` half of that equality holds for a PREBUILT group only — a from-source group is a COMPILE and may consume several signed tarballs, held together by the URL-keyed anchor rule instead — while one `cache_version` and one `acquisition` per group stay mode-independent.] §3.7.2 item 4's sub-component rows (libimagequant) follow the same law with the hash ANCHORING the pinned source instead of staged bytes. `available` is an optional **scalar** bool on a codec row.     > `corroboration_urls`). The fetch per-hop rule becomes: https + hostname ∈ (provenance ∪
    > transport) for that engine; integrity comes from `asset_sha256`, the transport set only
    > bounds egress. INTERIM posture (until this artifact lands) is deliberately fail-closed: the
    > row-named hosts are provenance-only, so a redirect-to-CDN pin simply cannot be fetched yet —

The parsers they share with this bin live in a flat `xtask/src/lib.rs` library target, because a module declared from `main.rs` is private to that bin target. Never `xtask/src/bin/` or any nested directory under `xtask/`: a new tracked directory fails G69 (plan-lint check 26), and its only fix edits the fingerprinted §0.7 tree, an owner-acked precondition. Only this bin keeps a `[[bin]]` target, because §6.4.3a pins its `scripts/` source path. **Core-access door [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** `convertia-core` is minimal-pub (§0.7 lib.rs row), so xtask reaches the §0.6 types and the `engines::lock` schema through a read-only `pub mod gate_api` behind the non-default `gate-api` cargo feature (the `fuzz_api` pattern). xtask depends on `convertia-core = { path = "../src-tauri", features = ["gate-api"] }` plus its own `toml`. A row or column label is the cell text with `**`, the footnote marks (`†` `*` `¹` `²`) and any trailing `(.ext)` parenthetical removed. It resolves to its `UserFacingFormat` variant through ONE label table shared with the `covers` reader (e.g. `MPG/MPEG`→`Mpeg`, `3GP`→`ThreeGp`); an unresolvable label FAILS. A diagonal cell is one whose row label equals its column label (the video matrix is not square), excluded whatever it holds. An off-diagonal cell is a required pair iff its text begins with `✓` or `★`, and not a pair iff it begins with `—` or `out`. video.md's `→audio` and `→GIF` columns are a view owned by cross-category.md and are skipped. The two cross-category ops come from cross-category.md: the sources are the ✓ rows of its `Video source ＼ operation` table, the to-GIF target is `GIF`, and the extract-audio targets are every row of its `Target subset offered` table, the `[DEFER: corpus]` rows included. Resolving `[OPEN-A]` against a target deletes its table row in the same commit, and the guard follows. Generator built here; pairs marked reliable category-by-category in P5–P7. Also **WIRES G72** (engines.lock-bump corpus re-validation) — a named deliverable of this box, not a side effect (the build-gates G72 row reads "Wired in P4.61"; no G72 enforcement exists in the tree): a `scripts/check-*` trailer gate over the push range (the G71/`check-l-neg1-ack` mechanism pattern) asserting every commit changing an `engines.lock` engine-version/SHA row carries a `Reliability-Gate: <ledger-ref>` attestation (the trailer ruling below), + its **G24 positive+negative self-test** registered in `scripts/gate-selftests/` (a post-P4 version-row edit without the trailer ⇒ MUST fail; with it ⇒ MUST pass; a non-`engines.lock` diff ⇒ MUST pass; a pure new-row addition without the trailer ⇒ MUST pass; a `triples` split that re-pins an existing `(id, triple)` into a new row at a different version, without the trailer ⇒ MUST fail; an existing row removed or its `id` renamed, without the trailer ⇒ MUST fail), + the lefthook L2 / ci.yml L4 wiring, + the build-gates G72-row Delivered update with the row's trigger sentence and self-test list reconciled to the trigger below. It lands **fail-closed directly** — the P1–P3 fail-soft window was the gate's ABSENCE (held by the roles §5a Co-Pilot hold, which lifts when the owner-acked G72 act lands), so there is NO existing gate-planes `[[fail_open]]` row to remove; a `[[fail_open]]`/`[[posture_flag]]` row joins `gate-planes.toml` only if the script itself ships a bootstrap leg. Every wiring surface (`scripts/check-*`, `scripts/gate-selftests/**`, `lefthook.yml`, `.github/**`, `scripts/*.toml`, `docs/security/**`) is **L(-1)**. **Pre-declared owner tail (build-loop §6, roles §4(g)) [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** it reds nothing until it lands, so it is neither a hard-stop nor an `[!extern]` precondition. The Loop builds the ledger generator green and flips this box `[x]` on that Loop half. The G72 wiring above is ONE owner-acked act (`L-neg1-ack: owner`, G71) in the P4.81 caged-tail list. **Harness-fixture carve-out (mirrors P4.60/P4.79):** the flagged `harness_fixture` diagonal (the P4.79 PNG→PNG exit fixture) is emitted as an INFORMATIONAL cell outside the release-gate enumerated set — the release predicate ignores it, so the first ledger cell over the P4 fixture is producible without widening the §04-enumerated release-gate set (per the §6.5.2 informational-row clause, Co-Pilot pre-fill spec edit 2026-09-15).
  > **Realization note (pre-decided so the fill does not fork, the P4.60 sibling pattern):** the generator is the xtask task `reliability-ledger` (`cargo run -p xtask -- reliability-ledger`) in a flat module under `xtask/src/`, per the P4.60 sibling-guard realization. Its input is the P4.59 runner record authored into §6.5.2. A patent-gapped pair's `unavailable-per-§3.4` cell comes from that runner outcome, and the generator never re-reads `engines.lock` (§3.4.4a: no second place the posture is decided). The release-gate cell set calls the `xtask/src/lib.rs` required-pair enumeration P4.60 homes, never a re-implementation. The output is `reliability-report.json` + the human table, folded over one or more legs' records into `(source, target, platform)` cells, with the §6.5.2 release predicate computed in the JSON. The three-leg Lane-B run and merge are P10.61's; publication is P10.58's.
  > **G72 trigger (the bump semantics of §6.5.4, roles §5a and the G72 row):** per commit in the range, the gate parses `src-tauri/engines.lock` in the parent and in the commit and keys every row by `(id, triple)` for each `triples` entry (the §3.7.2 row law). It never diffs `version`/`sha256` lines, because a `triples` split can move an existing triple into a new row at a new version with no removed `version` line. It fires iff a parent key is missing in the commit or has a different effective `version`, `cache_version` (defaults to `version`), `source_ref`, `sha256`, `asset_sha256` or `from_source.tarball_sha256`. A commit that only adds keys passes: the P4.89 and P5–P7 staging rows are validated by their category ledger boxes. An unparseable side fails closed. Range resolution reuses `check-l-neg1-ack`'s `resolve_base` / `commits_in_range`.
  > **`Reliability-Gate` trailer [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** option (c): a shape-only attestation trailer, the G71 pattern. No doc defines `<ledger-ref>`, and under single-branch `main` no three-platform proof run exists before a bump lands. The green three-platform check stays in the release pipeline. The fill records the trailer regex here; the G72 row wording, vuln-response §2 step 5 and roles-and-escalation §5a (its hold window and its "regenerated-green" proof wording) follow in this box's G72 owner tail.
  > **`demoted` source [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** option B: `docs/demoted-pairs.md` itself is the committed input the generated ledger reads. The generator reads an absent `docs/demoted-pairs.md` as zero demotions, and it fails on a demoted row that names no §04 cell or platform. It needs no new file and no §0.7 row. This box's fill commit restates §6.5.3's source/projection sentence (DoD 2), and the `demoted` halves of P10.42 and P11.16 become true by construction.

## Binary-size-budget levers (§3.9)

- [ ] **P4.62** [BUILD] Build the early per-component size-baseline measurement (compressed, per platform) · §3.9 §3.9.1 §3.9.2
  needs: P4.27
  > the §3.9.2 measurement ("the real numbers are measured in §6.1 once the trimmed engine builds exist, and fed back here"), taken early as an incremental baseline so P5–P7 track size cost against the ≤400 MB compressed ceiling rather than discovering overflow at release. No spec § mandates the early take; it is this plan's sequencing choice. Measure the per-component compressed contribution per platform (LibreOffice, fonts, FFmpeg+libs, image stack, poppler, pandoc, the app); the macOS universal near-doubling note. The §6.7.2 release-time size GATE (G41) itself is P10; the levers + baseline are owned here.
  > **[Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** the §3.9 intro "no hard size cap" literal and the §3.9.1 fonts-row `[OPEN — §3.9.2]` were reconciled to the §3.9.2 and §3.9.3 `[DECIDED]` text, so this box's read set no longer disagrees with itself.
  > **Shape [pre-fill audit 2026-09-15; decided here]:** (1) The record is a committed `src-tauri/size-baseline.toml` beside `engine-configure.toml` (no new directory). It holds one table per (`cache_engine` group | `app`) × triple, carrying `installed_bytes`, `compressed_bytes` and the measuring commit. A new `scripts/stage-engines --measure-sizes [--target T]` mode writes it. (2) `compressed_bytes` is a declared PROXY, never the artifact figure: stdlib DEFLATE (`zipfile` `ZIP_DEFLATED`, `compresslevel=9`) per file, summed. The §3.9.2 artifact size stays P10.36's (G41), and the proxy is claimed conservative for no format unless measured. (3) It measures the cache bytes the byte-free `--check` resolution selects per triple (the P4.27 `--root`/`--cache-root`/`--target` seam), never a staged tree. Staging a macOS or Linux triple needs host-only `lipo`/`install_name_tool`/`otool`/`patchelf`. (4) `universal-apple-darwin` follows the `merge_universal_tree` rule without `lipo`: a byte-identical pair counts once, a differing Mach-O pair counts both slices, never the plain sum of the two slice figures. (5) At P4 the record holds no engine rows (`STAGED_ENGINES = ()`). The `app` row comes from a release `convertia-core` binary for each triple the measuring host can build, and stays informational, outside the check. (6) Catcher: `--check`, which `ci.yml` already runs, compares `STAGED_ENGINES` × declared triples with the baseline engine rows in two directions [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]. The orphan-row direction (a baseline row for an undeclared engine or triple) fails closed on every run, because it needs no bytes, and it runs before `main`'s early return for an empty `STAGED_ENGINES`. The missing-row direction is scoped to the triples whose cache entries this run resolves, because the CI `--check` job restores no cache and non-host from-source entries exist only on the native compile runners; the missing-row catcher is the staging box's local `--check` run. A declared triple without a resolved entry is reported as unmeasured, never passed as measured; that report is the posture here, and the shipped-artifact size is measured at release by P10.36 (G41). Each P5–P7 staging box therefore re-runs `--measure-sizes` and commits its rows in the same commit. The legs join the `--selftest` suite.
  > **[Pre-declared owner-acked tails — named with the box; neither is a precondition; both close in the P4.81 caged-tail list]:** (a) the §0.7 physical-tree row for `src-tauri/size-baseline.toml` plus its `spec-0.7-physical-tree` fingerprint re-bless, one owner act as for the `engine-configure.toml` row. G69 asserts directories only, so the file lands green without the row. (b) the planted positive from outside the tool in `g24-stage-engines.py` for the new `--check` baseline-binding guard (the P4.29.1/P4.30 pattern). Added `--selftest` legs do not red the monotone leg-name pin. No `.github/**` edit is owed: the guard rides the existing `--check` invocation.

> names the P3 box it supersedes + carries the `needs: P3.5x` edge, so the loop builds the

The handler is a `pub async fn` returning `Result<(), IpcError>` and runs three steps in order. **(1)** `RunRegistry::cancel(runId)`, the same trip C7 performs; an unknown or finished `runId` is a no-op that goes straight to (3). **(2)** a BOUNDED wait for the run to leave the registry (`has_active_run() == false` after the conductor's `finish`). The bound is the §1.7 group-kill timeout, because exit never blocks on a wedged descendant (§7.3.2). `RunRegistry` has no await primitive, so polling or a notify-on-`finish` is a `[Build-Session-Entscheidung: P4.67.1]`. **(3)** `app.exit(0)`, which the P2.137 quit-leg guard never blocks. Stay and Esc invoke nothing. G23 is untouched: C15 is not a conversion command. **Rejected:** a `core:window:allow-close` grant with a WebView `close()` — an L(-1) capability change against §0.10 `[DECIDED]`, and `close()` re-raises `CloseRequested` into the busy guard, handing the wind-down wait to JS (the split §7.3.2 `[REC]` forbids).

## Resource pre-flight & budgets engine (§1.10)

> The §1.10 `[DECIDED design]` estimation+decision mechanism — the cross-cutting home
> is P4 (every engine phase depends on it; P4 owns the §0.9 pool + §2.14 staging it
> composes with). P2 DECLARED the `PreflightVerdict` DTO + C4 RETURNS it; P3 FEEDS it
> the walking-skeleton verdict; P5/P6 FEED inputs (SVG clamp, to-GIF estimate). These
> two boxes BUILD the engine those declarations/feeds resolve against; P9.41 then
> VALIDATES + calibrates the numbers against the corpus.

P4.24 verified no such per-item reclaim existed and homed it on **P4.88**, which is why that box is in this one's `needs:` — the dependency, not this note, is the enforcement. Without the reclaim the true footprint is the whole-batch Σ while a peak-concurrent estimate would UNDER-count, i.e. pass a preflight that then exhausts the system volume mid-run (the failure §2.14.4 exists to prevent) — and modelling Σ instead is NOT an available fallback: §2.14.2 `[DECIDED]` forbids it ("**not** the sum of every item's staged-input size across the whole batch, which would over-count and falsely trip `OutOfDisk`") and this box's own scope line says the same. (c) A subprocess engine's disk-full becomes `OutOfDisk` without per-engine code. The batch continues through the existing per-item `Failed` arm (§1.9/§1.11). The commit's spec sync (DoD 2) adds the T10 output/scratch byte budget to §1.10: the N×-input ratio with its per-item floor, the per-item output ceiling, the distinct per-item scratch ceiling, the kill to `Failed(TooBig)`, the leg-(c) free-space margin, and finite `[DEFER: corpus]` starting values. It also reconciles the §3.5.1 "everything else" stderr line with leg (c).   > **Byte budget [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** (i) `N = 1000` with a per-item floor of 1 GiB, so the budget is max(N × input, floor); both are `[DEFER: corpus]`. (ii) *Amended at the 2b review:* each item's engine runs with its working files (its §2.12.3(a) cwd, its temp dir and its per-invocation LibreOffice profile, §3.5.2) in a per-item sub-directory of the run's kind-2 dir. The §3.5.0 staged source copy is input, so the poll never counts it. The leg-(a) scratch comparison covers that sub-directory, so a scratch bomb ends `Failed(TooBig)` as T10 and P9.41 expect. §1.7 sets `cwd` and the temp env to the per-item directory after planning, as it fills `out_tmp`; P7.8, the LibreOffice invocation shell whose per-job `--outdir` is the first argv-embedded per-item path, adds the plan-time input for such paths. The item's working sub-directory is removed at its terminal transition, beside the P4.88 staged-copy reclaim. (iii) *Amended at the 2b review:* the leg-(c) free-space watchdog kills the largest attributable consumer first, as `Failed(OutOfDisk)` and one item per poll interval, so concurrent items are not failed together. Every item's watchdog poll updates a run-scoped table of attributable bytes, and one arbiter reads that table to choose the single victim per interval; the home of the table and the arbiter is a `[Build-Session-Entscheidung: P4.73]`. The one-victim leg is vacuous against the sequential conductor until P4.86 lands (at most one item is in flight), so it is driven against a hand-built concurrent set, never the live conductor (the P4.88 leg-(2) rule). It still replaces per-engine `classify_failure` out-of-disk spellings, so no P5–P7 engine box gains a `needs: P4.73`.

## The §7.2.5 orphan-reclaim slot body — sweep_stale at startup (deferred from P2.106.5)

> P2.106.5 built §7.2 step 5 as the scratch+log dir creation with an `Ok(())` orphan-reclaim SLOT
> ("mechanism §2.6, body P3/P4") — and no P4 box named the body, leaving `crate::run::sweep_stale`'s
> intended §7.2.5 startup caller owner-less (the P3.74 [Decision] FLAG, resolved 2026-07-21 by
> authoring this box). Appended at end-of-phase (`.82`, the max+1 id) per the established convention.

- [ ] **P4.82** [RUST] Fill the §7.2.5 orphan-reclaim slot body — `prepare_scratch_and_log` → `crate::run::sweep_stale` before the window shows, outcome logged per §7.5, never silent-clean + the startup scratch-writability probe raising `ScratchUnavailable` · §7.2.5 §7.2.1 §2.6 §2.13.3 §2.8 §0.4.2
  needs: P2.106.5, P3.23
  > the §7.2.1-step-5 body the P2.106.5 SLOT reserved (the crate-root `prepare_scratch_and_log` — lib.rs since the P3.87 split — an `Ok(())` slot; the run/mod.rs caller note still carries the old `main::` phrasing, corrected when this box touches it): call `crate::run::sweep_stale` over the launch's `app_local_data_dir()` scratch base — reclaim DEAD prior/foreign runs' central `run-<RunId>/` dirs, held-lock-gated (§2.6.3: never a concurrent instance's live temp; panic-free by construction) — BEFORE the window shows (§7.2.5 owns the WHEN; §2.6 owns the mechanism), logging the reclaim outcome via the §7.5 logger under its redaction rules (a cleanup that cannot complete is logged, never reported as a clean success — §7.2.5). §7.2.5's "and surfaced per §2.6" is that same §7.5 log line here, not an unbuilt UI half: every §2.6.4 surface is keyed to an item or its run, and the step-5 sweep runs before either exists. The log line names the dir's structural `InstanceId`/`RunId` at the default level and the full path only in verbose (§7.5.3). Tag the call site `[Derived-Assumption: P4.82 — §2.6.4 surfaces are per-item; the startup could-not-reclaim surface is the §7.5 log line]`. Idempotent with the P3.74 `RunEvent::Exit` backstop (same sweep, same lock gate — the two callers never conflict; the run/mod.rs caller note names both). The destination-resident `*.part` reclaim stays §2.6.3(b)/P3.24 (opportunistic-by-later-write), NOT this startup call; a sweep error is best-effort, never an `AppFault`. The §7.2.5 cannot-complete leg is NOT reachable from the caller with the as-built primitive (`sweep_stale` returns only the REMOVED dirs; a Remove-verdict dir whose `remove_dir_all` fails is silently absent from the return — run/mod.rs, P3.23). This box therefore EXTENDS `sweep_stale`/`sweep_stale_within` to also surface the failed reclaims (e.g. a `SweepOutcome { removed, failed }` return), so the STARTUP caller logs both reclaimed and could-not-reclaim dirs via the §7.5 facade under its redaction rules. Do NOT log inside the sweep body: the P3.74 `RunEvent::Exit` caller runs AFTER the log flush (§7.3.2 order — flush logs, then best-effort cleanup; lib.rs P3.74), so an internal warn would emit post-flush; that caller keeps its P3.74 best-effort drop-the-result posture unchanged (`drop(…)` still satisfies the lib.rs `sweep_stale` source-scan needle). The affected `run::sweep_tests` assertions update mechanically in the same commit under the test-strategy §8 [Test-Change] discipline; `run/mod.rs` is loop-editable (not L(-1)). (`needs: P2.106.5` for the slot + `P3.23` for `sweep_stale` — the cross-phase edge wired here per the P4.77 reconciliation obligation.)
  > **Startup writability probe [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]:** §2.13.3's "no writable scratch" startup class is raised HERE, as §2.13.3 writes it. At §7.2.1 step 5 this box creates the per-instance scratch root, then writes and deletes a probe file inside a `.lock`-held `run-<probe RunId>/` taken through `RunScratch::acquire` and released by `cleanup_run` (the P4.44.1 pattern), so a concurrent sweep cannot remove the shell and a crash residue is reclaimable. A failure returns the NEW app-level kind `ScratchUnavailable`. Its §7.2-owned calm string points at free space and permissions and never tells the user to download again. Only the probe raises an `AppFault`; a sweep error stays best-effort. The same commit syncs §7.2.1's `[DECIDED]` readiness split, §2.8.1 / §2.8.2, §0.4.2's `app://fault` row, §0.4.3 `ErrorKind`, §2.13.3, §7.1.2's RunId row (its exception becomes "the §7.2 startup probes: the §7.2.3 smoke probe and the §7.2.1 step-5 writability probe") and §7.2.5 ("per-instance root probed at startup through a probe-scoped run dir; per-run subdirs created on first need"), and every site listing the app-level kinds, found by grep (e.g. §3.2.2, `src/components/AppFaultNotice.tsx`, `src/lib/ipc/events.ts` and the `lib.rs` readiness-gate doc comments naming `EngineMissing`/`BundleDamaged`). It carries `[Test-Change]` on the three-variant wire pins and the `complete_kind_list!` rosters, plus an `xtask codegen` regen. P4.50 presents the kind.

## The §2.14.2 per-item staged-copy reclaim — the P4.24-raised plan gap

> One item raised at P4.24 while its §6.4.2 kill-fence test was being verified (that box's note carries
> the evidence): §2.14.2 is `[DECIDED]` that the macOS staged source copy is reclaimed **per item** as
> each engine finishes, and no box owned it — neither reclaimer that exists is per-item (the §2.6.2
> run-scope cleanup fires at run end, the §2.6.3 sweep at the NEXT launch), so the §1.10
> peak-concurrent bound that rests on the per-item clause is not yet true. Appended at end-of-phase (`.88`, the
> max+1 convention); authored by the **Build-Loop 2026-08-30**, which is why it sits in its own section
> rather than under the P4.20 one above (that section's heading, its count and its authorship line all
> scope it to the two Co-Pilot-authored `.86`/`.87` boxes).

- [ ] **P4.88** [RUST] Reclaim the macOS staged source copy PER ITEM as its engine finishes, so the §2.14.2 peak-concurrent kind-2 bound is true · §2.14.2 §2.6 §1.7 · G31
  needs: P4.25
  > **Raised by P4.24 (2026-08-30), which found this normative obligation owned by no box.** §2.14.2 is `[DECIDED]` that *"staged source copies are reclaimed **per item** as each engine finishes, so at most §0.9 **concurrency-degree** of them coexist"*, and it is the SOLE justification for §1.10 bounding the macOS staged-input term to `~degree × largest in-flight staged inputs` instead of the whole-batch Σ. P4.24 delivered the §2.14.2 *lifecycle* half — created-after-the-run-lock (structural), reclaimed by the §2.6.2 run-scope cleanup, and reclaimed after a crash by the §2.6.3 startup sweep (the §6.4.2 kill fence, tested) — but not one of those is PER-ITEM (two fire at run end, the sweep at the next launch), so today a whole batch's staged copies coexist for the length of the run and the peak-concurrent claim is false. THIS BOX: delete the staged copy at the item's TERMINAL transition — success, failure AND cancel alike, on the §1.7 invocation path that owns "the engine finished" — never leaving it to end-of-run, and never before the engine's last read. The §2.6 reclaimers stay as the crash/cancel backstop; this adds the in-run half they cannot cover. Test at §6.4.2 level: (1) no staged copy survives its item's terminal transition on ANY of the three outcomes — the load-bearing leg, non-vacuous the moment this box lands; and (2) at most `degree` staged copies exist at any instant across a batch larger than `degree`, the assertion that makes the §1.10 bound honest. **Leg (2) is VACUOUS until P4.86 lands** (the tier-1 conductor is sequential until then, so at most ONE item is ever in flight and the bound holds for a reason unrelated to the reclaim) — written down here because P4.24's own review lost a round to exactly this shape, a guard test that passed for the wrong reason. Either build leg (2) after P4.86 or assert it against a hand-driven concurrent set, never against the live sequential conductor. **Blocks P4.72** (`needs: P4.88`): §2.14.2 `[DECIDED]` forbids the Σ fallback, so there is no correct §1.10 model until this lands.
  > **A P4.25 test asserts the residue THIS box removes — inverting it is expected, not a suppression. [Raised by P4.25]** P4.25's macOS-only §6.4.3 leg `on_macos_the_conductor_stages_the_source_into_the_run_scratch_before_planning` drives the real `convert_item` and asserts the staged copy is STILL in the test-owned `RunScratch` afterwards — which is only true because no per-item reclaim exists yet, i.e. it asserts the current spec-correct state and is what proves P4.25's wiring at all. When this box lands, that assertion inverts (the copy must be GONE once the item is terminal) and the edit carries `[Test-Change: P4.88 — old-obsolete+new-correct, §2.14.2]`: the old expectation is obsolete because §2.14.2 `[DECIDED]` licenses exactly this box to end the residue, and the new one is correct by the same clause. Named here so the edit is a planned consequence rather than a test that mysteriously goes red.
  > **DOC-SYNC THIS BOX OWNS (DoD 2), and the adjudication behind it.** Two spec texts still describe the OLD model and must be corrected in **this box's** commit, not earlier — P4.24 deliberately wrote no spec text, because it builds no reclaim and a spec saying "per item" ahead of any code doing it is DoD item 2 inverted. **(a)** §3.5.0 step 3 currently reads *"the staged scratch source is reclaimed with the run (§2.6)"* → *"reclaimed **per item** as the engine finishes (§2.14.2), with the §2.6 run-scope cleanup and the §2.6.3 startup sweep as backstops"* (every proposition it asserts is already stated in §2.14.2 — the "backstop" relation RENDERS §2.14.2's own *"It is **also** reclaimed by the §2.6.2 run-scope cleanup"*, so the sentence is a citation correction rather than authorship of the §-owner's string; that one word is the adjudication's reading of "also", not spec wording). **(b)** §2.6.2's `[DECIDED]` trigger table needs the staged-copy row — deferred to here because it requires deciding WHICH triggers fire (success / failure / cancel), which is this box's call. **Leave §2.14.2 alone:** it is the single owner and already correct.
  > **THE ADJUDICATION (P4.24 review, 2026-08-30) — recorded because the reviewers SPLIT and the owner may overturn it.** One reviewer first raised §3.5.0-step-3-vs-§2.14.2 as a **P0 spec-internal contradiction**, i.e. CLAUDE.md's unconditional hard-stop. On adjudication BOTH reviewers independently concluded it is **NOT** one, by TWO INDEPENDENT ROUTES, both recorded here so neither looks like a refutation of the other later. **Route 1 — CHRONOLOGY:** §3.5.0 step 3 was authored at `def9dac` 2026-06-13 **13:32**, the §2.14.2 peak-concurrent invariant at `29ecc65` the same day **20:03** — the coarse text is 6.5 h OLDER and was simply never swept forward when the invariant landed; §2.6.2's table covers the kind-2 run dir only at the RUN level (its "Run end" and "Next app start" rows both name `run-<RunId>/`); every ITEM-level row is pure kind-1 vocabulary (`renameat2`, the partial `tmp`), so there is no item-level kind-2 row — which is exactly why no staged-copy trigger row exists to cite. **Beware the tooling trap that nearly discredited this:** a bare `git log -S` reports the 2026-06-14 `docs/` MOVE commit for **both** files (measured: identical `1f9ead0 09:54:28` on `02-guarantees` and on `03-engines`), so it ERASES the ordering rather than inverting it — the spurious "step 3 is newer" reading came from comparing a bare `-S` result on one file against a rename-following result on the other. `-S` cannot see past a rename without `--follow` (`git blame` follows on its own), so use the SAME method on both sides. **Route 2 — TEXT/OWNERSHIP,** which stands without any archaeology: (i) §2.14 is tagged `[DECIDED — single owner here]` for temp/scratch lifecycle while §3.5.0 opens by disclaiming it (*"Requirement owner: §7.2.6. This subsection owns the engine-arg / handle plumbing"*), so step 3's "(§2.6)" is a stale **citation**, not a rival normative clause; (ii) inside the owning §, the general kind-2 rule (*"They are cleaned with the run"*) is followed by a `[DECIDED]` carve-out for the named THIRD contributor — general-then-specific specialisation, the ordinary reading rule; (iii) the clauses are JOINTLY SATISFIABLE (reclaim per item; `cleanup_run` still removes the dir on every exit; the startup sweep still covers a crash), and a contradiction requires that no single implementation satisfy both. The tie-breaker if one remained: the per-item clause has downstream normative DEPENDENTS (§2.14.4's macOS estimate, §1.10's bound are computed from it), while nothing computes anything from "reclaimed with the run". **The plan therefore follows the per-item model** — here, in P4.25's scope clause and in P4.72's `needs:`. If the owner overturns this, only plan prose reverts: P4.24's CODE is side-neutral (it creates the copy and reclaims nothing; its §6.4.2 kill fence asserts only the §2.6.3 startup sweep, which BOTH readings state).

## The P4.34 link-input acquisition — the `[!extern]` prerequisite the 2026-09-09 hard-stop surfaced

> Authored by the **Co-Pilot 2026-09-09** on the Build-Loop's roles §4(d)+(g) hard-stop at P4.34 (the
> P4.26 → P4.38 → P4.37 → P4.35 → P4.34 DECISION-C chain); max+1 convention. Its own section because the
> P4.34 box above is what it unblocks, and the sweep section below stays the file's last.

- [!extern] **P4.89** [BUILD,CI] Establish the P4.34 link-input acquisition — the owner-ruled §3.8 anchor for the image stack's unsigned upstreams, the owner-acked `engines.lock` rows for the imgworker's full static-link closure, the non-Linux `engine-asset-compile` legs + the `from_source.toolchain_digest` binding, the link-input output anchor, the consumer half in every compiling job, and the image structural reader · §3.8 §3.7.2 §6.1.3 §3.1 §6.4.3 · G37 G71 G31
  needs: P4.56.3
  > **[!extern] (owner + Co-Pilot — every deliverable is an L(-1) file or an owner policy call; the Build-Loop never edits `src-tauri/engines.lock` / `.github/**` / the spec §3.8 anchor set, G71):** P4.34 `needs:` this box. Deliverables (a) to (e), in order, each a dual-reviewed owner-acked act (a′, d and e added by the 2026-09-10 pre-fill audit; b′ by the 2026-09-15 ruling on P4.76):
  > **(a) The anchor ruling (owner — a roles §4(b)/(c) fork: the trust root of the from-source pins).** Under the 2026-07-22 ruling two groups compile from source (libvips, ImageMagick) and five rows are prebuilt-mode (libheif, libde265, librsvg, cgif, libimagequant); `FromSourceAnchor` (`crate::engines::lock`, required iff `acquisition = "from-source"`) implements §3.8's from-source anchor as authored — a detached signature over the source tarball, `from_source.signature_url` — so that anchor is the question for the from-source inputs, while the prebuilt rows rest on §3.8's prebuilt anchors (i) mirrors / (ii) signed-repo AS AUTHORED, no schema change (their open question is per-triple SOURCING, (b)). MEASURED 2026-09-09 against every current upstream release, all seven: **libvips** 8.18.6 publishes `vips-8.18.6.tar.xz` + a same-origin `.sha256sum` — no detached signature, no GitHub artifact attestation, and its release commit is signed only by GitHub's web-flow key (`B5690EEEBB952194`), i.e. no maintainer signature exists anywhere; **ImageMagick** 7.1.2-31's GitHub release publishes `ImageMagick-7.1.2-31.tar.xz` + `ImageMagick-7.1.2-31.intoto.jsonl` and two GitHub artifact attestations (in-toto / Sigstore) on the tarball digest, no `.asc`/`.sig` (imagemagick.org's versioned `/archive/` path answers 404 for the tarball and both signature spellings alike — secondary evidence only); **libheif** 1.23.4 and **libde265** 1.1.2 publish a bare `.tar.gz` on an unsigned commit; **librsvg** 2.63.0's GNOME release carries `librsvg-2.63.0.tar.xz` + a same-origin `.sha256sum` on download.gnome.org, its tag commit unsigned (GitLab reports no signature); **cgif** 0.5.4 has no release asset at all, its release commit signed by the maintainer's Ed25519 key (fpr `7C053C98C310311A5CBA1645547A7E1332669A19`); the **lovell/libimagequant** v2.4.1 tag is a signed TAG (fpr `0F4ABFEEF4E7AC702A66375B26C1635893ACB81F`) and the fork exists ONLY as that tag — no release asset, no distro package (Debian ships upstream 2.x) — so it is from-source by necessity on every triple and is the third from-source input (which corrects the 2026-07-22 ruling's placement of the fork on the P4.28 prebuilt path — hence the FOUR prebuilt rows the P4.34 note names). So the from-source anchor as authored is satisfiable for NONE of the three from-source inputs; the candidates, each a spec §3.8 + `lock.rs` schema + `scripts/compile-engine-asset` change: **(α)** the signed-VCS-tag route P0.7.3 already prefers — a `from_source` variant recording the tag/commit signature, verified with `git verify-tag` / `git verify-commit` against the pinned fingerprint, the source taken by `git archive` with `configure`/`m4` regenerated locally (covers libimagequant and cgif — NOT libvips or ImageMagick); **(β)** a Sigstore / GitHub-artifact-attestation variant (`gh attestation verify` as a third `verified_with` tool; covers ImageMagick); **(γ)** an independent-origin SOURCE corroboration — the upstream tarball's SHA-256 matched against a GPG-signed distro source index or an independent distro's pin of the identical bytes (§3.8's prebuilt anchors (i)/(ii) applied to a source input; measured: Debian's signed `.dsc` carries the upstream tarball VERBATIM for libheif 1.23.3, libde265 1.1.2 and cgif 0.5.4 — hash-equal to the GitHub asset / tag tarball — while its vips is a `.orig.tar.gz` repack of the upstream `.tar.xz` and its ImageMagick and librsvg are `+dfsg` repacks, content-level only; libvips 8.18.6's `.tar.xz` hash is pinned verbatim by Homebrew-core, `3c41e1d5…dda3e`, equal to the GitHub digest); **(δ)** the divergence inside §3.8's own from-source bullet: its parenthetical admits "GPG/minisign/SHA on the project's own site" as corroboration (the laundering clause sits in the PREBUILT bullet) while the same bullet mandates a detached-signature `from_source.signature_url` — the P4.28.1 schema implements that mandatory clause, so the permissive parenthetical and the schema disagree; the ruling either strikes the parenthetical (a same-origin hash proves only that one host is self-consistent) or relaxes BOTH the bullet's `signature_url` clause and the schema (a relaxation of a real check); the security argument admits a same-origin `.sha256sum` under neither reading. Same candidate, same act: §3.8 admits `minisign` while `verified_with` is exactly {`gpg`, `sq`} — a second spec↔schema gap the ruling closes in one direction or the other. The same anchor question is P5.1.1 / P5.5.1 / P5.9.1 / P6.1.1 / P7.17.1's (the seam-fill set the P4.28.1 coupling note enumerates) — settled ONCE here, one §3.8 change, not five. The same ruling fixes G37's in-repo trust-root artifact ("a committed keyring / fingerprint list on the engine-source allow-list"). Its shape follows the chosen candidate, and its home is the P4.56.3 allow-list file or a sibling `scripts/`-caged file. `scripts/compile-engine-asset` binds its verify to it (an explicit keyring or `GNUPGHOME`, never the runner's ambient keyring). The P4.56.3 gate gains the leg checking each from-source row's pinned key against it, with its G24 planted positive.
  > **(a′) The link-input OUTPUT anchor (owner — a `lock.rs` schema + §3.7.2 + G37 ruling, one act with (a)).** No `engines.lock` field hashes a from-source compile's OUTPUT or a prebuilt entry's UNPACKED tree per triple, so the P4.34 `build.rs` link-input verify has nothing to read. Rule ONE of: **(i)** a per-triple output anchor on the already-per-triple-split sub-component rows (a `from_source.output_sha256` or per-file manifest field; `lock.rs`, §3.7.2 item 4, the `engines.lock` row-law header, and G37's cache-restore clause extended to every restored entry a build consumes), written only after a realizability probe shows the digest-pinned container re-emits byte-stable `.a`/`.lib`/headers across two compiles per triple (§6.2.5 holds reproducibility best-effort); or **(ii)** no output pin — the consuming job compiles the link inputs from the verified `.src` entries in the same job, with no cache restore between compile and link. Either way (d) extends `check-ci-supply-chain` check (10): a job restoring `.engine-cache` that runs `cargo` or `tauri build` must run the sanctioned link-input verify between them (L(-1), with its planted-mismatch self-test).
  > **(b) The rows (owner-acked L(-1), `L-neg1-ack: owner`; every SHA-256 a hard Co-Pilot escalation, §3.7.2/§6.3.4).** The seven inputs the 2026-07-22 ruling names are the FORMAT-driving components, not the link closure: libvips is built against GLib/GObject (+ libffi, pcre2, zlib, expat) and the everyday-format codecs (libjpeg-turbo, libpng, libwebp, libtiff, + lcms2/libexif as configured), the §3.5.5 `rsvg::Loader` path pulls librsvg's text stack (cairo, pango, harfbuzz, freetype, fontconfig), and ImageMagick links the same base codec set — an ILLUSTRATIVE list from the libraries' own build requirements, the exact per-triple closure being this act's measurement — every one a statically-absorbed, separately-licensed component that spec §3.1's inventory (the SBOM granularity, "every one") does not enumerate. This act authors the row set for the WHOLE closure per triple — from-source groups (libvips + ImageMagick with their curated properties, per the P4.34 ruling; each dependency either compiled into the group's shared prefix as an earlier row, or a distro-signed prebuilt per §3.8 (ii)) with `kind = "sub-component"` rows for what the link absorbs (§3.7.2 item 4; G35a derives them from the link and fails on a missing row) — and reconciles spec §3.1 to the closure in the same act (spec > plan: the inventory's every-component clause is the higher source, so it gains the closure rows rather than the plan silently carrying them). The P4.56.3 origin allow-list gains every origin the rows name, in the same owner-acked commit as the rows. They are the manifest's first rows, and P4.56.3's fail-closed-on-first-row posture plus §3.8 [DECIDED] ("every `engines.lock` source URL … must resolve to an allow-listed origin") need the gate live before any row lands, so this box `needs:` P4.56.3. Its provenance set takes the upstream, signature and corroboration hosts; its transport set takes the redirect-CDN hosts. If (a) rules an anchor that adds a URL-bearing field, the same act extends the P4.56.3 gate to that field. Once P4.56.2 has added the mandatory `supplier` field (its field-map note), every row carries it; rows that land before it get `supplier` as P4.56.2's same-push owner tail. Two measurements this act owns before writing any prebuilt row: whether the distro `-dev` package for the triple ships the STATIC archive at all (Debian builds many libraries `--disable-static`; a shared-only package cannot feed a static link), and whether an MSVC-ABI prebuilt with a satisfiable anchor exists for `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` (MSYS2's signed repo ships MinGW-ABI packages, and libheif/libde265 are C++) — where either fails, that row is from-source on that triple (`acquisition` is a per-triple scalar, §6.1.3) and folds into (a). The same act records where the pinned libimagequant source's own `COPYRIGHT` text lands, which P4.54's COPYRIGHT leg reads (never the `third-party-licenses/` copy, P4.57). It also vendors each row's own licence/COPYING text into `third-party-licenses/` and regenerates the repo-root `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` / `NOTICE` pair in the same commit (P4.57's must-agree rule), so the P4.57 hook stays green [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn].
  > **(b′) The corresponding source of the prebuilt-mode LGPL rows (owner-acked L(-1), one per-triple act with (b)) [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn].** The prebuilt-mode LGPL rows of the static closure (libheif, libde265, librsvg, cgif) carry no `.src` cache entry, so nothing else acquires their complete corresponding source (§3.6.2). This act acquires it per triple beside the rows (b) writes and records where it lands, for P4.76's assertion and P10.21's assembly to read.
  > **(c) The CI half (owner-acked `.github/**`).** `engine-asset-compile` exists for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` only (the digest-pinned Debian container), and `engine-asset-plan` fails a from-source row that declares any other triple by design. The imgworker is an MSVC-target Rust binary on Windows (§3.3's `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`) and is built for both macOS slices, and `tauri-build` copies a declared sidecar at COMPILE time (P4.31's measured note) — so P4.34 needs the closure on all three OS in one act: the windows-2022 compile leg (the MSVC-ABI toolchain for a Meson/CMake stack — a roles §4(b) architecture call with no spec source, decided with (a)), the macos-14 leg for both slices (P4.29.1's declared cross table), the toolchain pins, the `from_source.toolchain_digest` binding P4.34's carry-list already owns, no canary tally bump (the P4.27 convention was re-cut 2026-09-09 to a monotone leg-name pin — an added `--selftest` leg reds nothing; the Co-Pilot re-blesses at the phase-end sweep), and the `ci.yml` `compile_triples` set. Until (c) lands, a from-source row for a non-Linux triple reds `main` at the plan step — so (b)'s non-Linux from-source rows and (c)'s legs land in the SAME push (or (b) lands the Linux-triple rows first); no intermediate state reds `main`.
  > **(d) The consumer half (owner-acked `.github/**` + check (10) + the dev-side recipe).** (a)–(c) put the link closure INTO the §6.1.3 cache; nothing yet takes it OUT where the workspace compiles. The root `Cargo.toml` is a virtual workspace with `crates/imgworker` as a member, so `check-rust-lint-contract` (`gate-tooling` on all three OS, lefthook L1 on every commit and L2 on every push) and `cargo llvm-cov --workspace` compile the imgworker and need its link closure; once P4.34 declares the sixth `externalBin` entry, every `convertia-core` compile — compile-sanity's `tauri build --no-bundle` on all three OS included — needs the placed sidecar before `tauri-build` runs. Today only the populate/compile jobs restore `.engine-cache`, and no step builds `-p convertia-imgworker`. This act lands before or with P4.34's `externalBin` entry (its steps red nothing while the entry is absent): **(i)** in `gate-tooling` and `compile-sanity` (and `fuzz.yml` once P4.35.1 imports the crate) a restore of the triple's link-closure entries, the (a′) verify before any link, then `cargo build -p convertia-imgworker` and placement under `src-tauri/binaries/` ahead of the first `convertia-core` compile; **(ii)** the placing actor — a Loop-built script outside `scripts/stage-engines` (check (10) refuses that pairing) or check (10) extended with the sanctioned step; **(iii)** the local-plane recipe: the Loop's machine runs the same clippy/test at L1/L2 with no Actions cache, so the dev-side acquisition (`fetch-engine-assets` + `compile-engine-asset` into a local `.engine-cache` for the host triple, on Windows with (c)'s MSVC-ABI toolchain) and the closure-discovery seam (`pkg-config` or an explicit prefix env — the spec is silent, a roles §4(b) call) are decided with (c) and recorded in §3.5.5 / §6.1.3 in the same act. `tauri.conf.json` `beforeBuildCommand` is not that recipe: it never fires for the `cargo clippy` / `cargo test` the gates run. The Lane-B release legs need the same build-and-place; P10.9 carries that forward-note.
  > **(e) The non-bundled §6.4.3 structural readers on the 3-OS `gate-tooling` job (P4.38's reader; every route is a `.github/**` or L(-1) act).** Of the §6.4.3 / G31 reader set only `ffprobe` and `pdftotext` are bundled. Measured routes for the image reader (`vipsheader` + `vips stats`): **(α)** the pinned from-source libvips build (a)–(c) already drive — libvips' Meson build emits `vips` / `vipsheader` unconditionally (v8.18.6 `tools/meson.build`) — restored into `gate-tooling` from the engine-asset cache, never staged into the bundle; **(β)** per-OS runner packages — Ubuntu 22.04 `libvips-tools` 8.12.1 (six minors behind the producer), Homebrew `vips` 8.18.6 (unpinned rolling), and on Windows only the upstream `vips-dev-x64-all` zip (no checksum or signature asset; `install-gate-tools` extracts one member while `vipsheader.exe` needs its DLL tree) — three trust postures for one oracle; **(γ)** a pinned pure-Rust decoder dev-dep reading dims and variance — PNG-only, and a same-commit reconcile of the `vipsheader` literal in §6.4.3, G31 and test-strategy §0.2. **[Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]: (α)**, the only route consistent with security-concept §0 pin-and-verify, its Windows leg riding (c)'s MSVC toolchain call. The same act names the box that provisions each remaining non-bundled reader (`pdfimages` for G31 (3), `unzip` on the Windows leg); `tesseract` has no v1 subject (P4.59, test-strategy §0.2 item 4).
  > **Not a P5.67 pull-forward:** P5.67 stays the owner of the P5-staged rows (the x265 plugin, libaom/dav1d) + the SBOM rows + the G35a derived closure and RE-VERIFIES these rows at their P5 station — never a second authoring (the P5.1.1/P5.5.1 mirror pattern; P5.67 carries the mirror). **Resumes:** this box `[x]` → P4.34 → P4.35 → P4.37 → P4.38 → P4.26 (DECISION C).

## The registry-driven §1.5 offer and option-declaration registry — the seam the option and lossy-note boxes read (the 2026-09-10 pre-fill audit)

> Authored by the **Co-Pilot 2026-09-15** from the test-strategy §11.4 pre-fill audit of P4.64.1: no box moves C3 and C6
> off the P3.48 walking-skeleton hard-wire, so the option gates, the lossy-note set and the P4 UX-harness leg have

  needs: P4.4, P2.8, P3.48, P3.49, P4.56.2
  > §1.5 sources the offered set and its `default` flag from the §3.2 registry, and §1.6 has `OptionDecl` "supplied by the registry". But §3.2.2 `EngineCapability` holds only `{source, target, direction}`, and C3 (`ipc/planning.rs` `resolve_targets`) and C6 (`ipc/conversion.rs` `resolve_slice_target`) still read the P3.48 hard-wire `engines::slice_target` (CSV↔TSV, `options: Vec::new()`). P4.4 re-cut only the conductor's select and plan.
  > **Deliver:** **(1)** the per-pair §0.6 `Target` data, declared through the §3.2 registry: the display `label`, the §1.5 rule-3 default flag, the §1.5 ≤1 `lossy` marker, `options: Vec<OptionDecl>`, and the §1.5 rule-5 offered-diagonal flag. Beside that data, not on `Target`, sits the per-pair §2.9.1 `LossyKind` set P4.65 renders, declared as registry data [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]. A `Some` `Target.lossy` marker's kind is a member of that pair's kind set, asserted by a registry unit test. `availability` comes from the §3.4 build-time capability filter. The data sits on the capability row or in a registry-side per-pair table (a `[Build-Session-Entscheidung: P4.92]`), one production source either way, and the additive §3.2.2 spec sync lands in this commit (DoD 2). The `String`-owning `OptionDecl` family is not const-constructible, so the lookup returns an owned `Vec` or a `LazyLock`-backed slice. CSV↔TSV registers as an explicit entry, so "no entry" and "an explicitly empty entry" are different states. **(2)** a pure, spawn-free per-source offer function over `engine_registry()` (`UserFacingFormat → Option<TargetOffer>`) applying §1.5 rules 1, 3 and 5, before any health marking; C3 `resolve_targets` delegates to it and then applies the health-cache marking (the consumer note below). **(3)** C6 validates the wire `TargetId` against the same offer. **(4)** `slice_target` and `resolve_slice_target` are deleted; `ipc/slice_round_trip.rs` and the orchestrator test mirrors move to the offer function in the same commit. **(5)** the registry-level offer function (before health marking) and the `UserFacingFormat` roster are exported through the P4.60 `gate_api` façade for the Lane-A guards [Co-Pilot ruling 2026-09-15 — owner may overturn]: the façade exports read-only types and pure lookup functions, never a `toml` parse, and a guard never reads the health cache.
  > **Consumer:** the degraded-target marking is P4.45's, which `needs:` this box: on every C3 call the C3 path marks a §3.1 degraded target `Target.availability` `Unavailable { reason }` from the updatable health cache, over the (2) offer, so the deferred macOS check (P4.46.1) reaches the next Confirm→Targets advance.
  > **Acceptance:** the CSV↔TSV offer comes out byte-identical, with the P3.49 C3 unit tests unchanged (G70). P5–P7 register their per-pair `Target` data and `OptionDecl` sets through this seam (the P5.74 / P6.92 / P7.77 reconciliation boxes carry the edge). In-phase consumers: P4.45, P4.60.2, P4.64, P4.64.1, P4.65, P4.79.
